# The knowledge base

The full working record of the project: **107 sections, about 6650 lines**, written as the
work happened rather than afterwards. It is not a paper and does not read like one. It is
here because it is the single most useful file in the repository for anyone continuing the
work, for one reason:

> **Most of it is about things that did not work.**

The packages in [`verifications/`](verifications/) tell you what is true. This tells you what
was tried, in what order, why each idea looked promising, and what killed it. That is the
part that is normally lost, and it is what stops the next person spending a week on an avenue
that was closed on day two.

The authoritative current statements are [`RESULTS.md`](RESULTS.md), which is generated by
`audit.py`, and the package READMEs. Where this file disagrees with them, they win.

### Reading it

The headings are numbered 1 to 108 with **no section 87** — a number was skipped, not a
section removed, and nothing in the repository refers to it. That is why the count above is
107 and the last heading reads 108.

It is roughly chronological, so later sections supersede earlier ones. Where they conflict,
**the later section wins** — and where a section is superseded, it usually says so. Some
specific warnings:

- §22 and §21 give dimension-45 and dimension-46 numbers that predate the mixed-moment fix of
  §58. Use §58 and later.
- §44's summary table lists dimension 19 as OPEN; §72 closes it.
- §60's dimension-44 figure predates §61.
- Several sections quote Cohn's table as of the date in their heading. It moved twice during
  the project.
- **Sections written before 2026-08-22 count dimensions 46 and 47 among the results.
  They are not claims, and removing them took that era's count from 46 to 44. The
  current count is 47 claims -- dimensions 62, 63, 68, 69 and 96 came later -- so no
  count in a dated section is comparable with today's. `RESULTS.md` is the authority.**
  Dimensions 46 and 47 were dropped on 2026-08-22: 23 766 960 is Boyvalenkov-
  Cherkashin, *Results in Mathematics* **80** (2025), Paper No. 3, equation (3) of
  the preprint arXiv:2312.05121, and
  12 309 600 is Table 3 of Sun-Wang, arXiv:2607.20359. Both were reached here
  independently and are kept in
  [`verifications/recovered/`](verifications/recovered/) as validation of the
  cross-section method, but neither is claimed. Sections written before that date
  discuss them as results; they are not.

The authoritative current statements are the package READMEs and
[`../RESULTS.md`](RESULTS.md), which is generated from `audit.py`.

**File paths in it are the working tree's, not this repository's.** It refers to scripts as
`research/dim38-39/...`, `research/class/...` and so on, 73 times — those are locations in
the author's working directory, which is not distributed (it is 5 GB, most of it
intermediate `.npy` files). Where a script it names is part of a claim, that script is here
under [`../verifications/`](verifications/); the map is:

### The thread that was open, and how it closed

§§73–75 reduced **dimension 17** to a single integer α and bracketed it to 192 ≤ α ≤ 193 —
τ(17) either 5730 (Cohn–Li's record) or **5732** (a new one). §97 settles it: **α = 192**, so
τ(17) = 5730 and there is no record here. The low range now has nothing left open.

The reason it took so long is worth keeping. §75 was revised *downward* after it was first
written, from "DIMENSION 17 IS CLOSED" to the bracket, because the CP-SAT run that reached 192
used 8 workers and did not reproduce — three runs of the same model gave 192, 193 and 195, and
the deterministic single-worker variant gave 238. The fix was not more solver time. `K'` is
closed under XOR, so `Cay(K', W_4)` is a translation scheme and Delsarte's LP bound applies;
it returns 192 exactly, and its dual is six rational numbers checkable in integer arithmetic.
[`../verifications/closed/dim17-layered-family/`](verifications/closed/dim17-layered-family/)
has the proof in `scripts/theta17.py`.

# Kissing numbers: working knowledge base

Started 2026-08-19. All experiments live in `research/`. Nothing here touches
`kissing27-verification/` (the finished dim-27 deliverable).

---

## 1. The master reduction for dimensions 25-31

Every record configuration in dims 25-31 (Cohn's table; attributed to Ma et al. 2025
"PackingStar", previously Kallal-Kan-Wang 2016, Cohn-Jiao-Kumar-Torquato 2011)
has ONE shape. Write `d = 24 + k`, `R^d = R^24 + R^k`.

| family  | points                                              | count |
|---------|-----------------------------------------------------|-------|
| equator | `(y, 0)`, `y` a Leech minimal vector                 | 196560 - (deleted) |
| caps    | `(sqrt(2/3)*u, sqrt(1/3)*w)`, `w in W` a unit vector in `R^k` | sum of `|U_w|` |
| axis    | `(0, a)`, `a in A` a unit vector in `R^k`            | `|A|` |

Everything is forced by the 60 degree condition (all derivations verified by hand):

* **cap level is forced.** Writing a cap as `(alpha*u, beta*w)`, the head threshold is
  `<u,u'> <= (1/2 - beta^2)/(1 - beta^2)`. For triples of directions to be allowed one
  needs `beta^2 >= 1/3`; for the head threshold to stay at 1/4 one needs
  `beta^2 <= 1/3`. So `beta^2 = 1/3` exactly. **No freedom here.**
* **same direction:** `<u,u'> <= 1/4`. Leech inner products (norm 32) are
  `{0,+-8,+-16,+-32}` i.e. cos in `{0,+-1/4,+-1/2,+-1}`, so the condition is exactly
  **"no two heads at inner product 16"**.
* **two directions sharing a head:** `<w,w'> <= -1/2`. At most 3 unit vectors are
  pairwise at cos <= -1/2, so a head set serves at most 3 directions ("a triple", and
  then the three directions sum to zero).
* **different triples:** `<w,w'> <= 1/2`, so `W` is a 60 degree code in `R^k`,
  `|W| <= tau_k`; and head sets on different triples must be **disjoint**.
* **equator vs cap:** only `y = u` violates, so delete each head from the equator, once.
* **axis:** `<a,w> <= sqrt3/2` (>= 30 deg), `<a,a'> <= 1/2`, so `|A| <= tau_k`, attained.

### The formula

    N(24+k) = 196560 + 2 * (#triples) * s + tau_k,      s = |U| = class size

with the published/current values

| k | dim | tau_k | W           | triples | s   | total  |
|---|-----|-------|-------------|---------|-----|--------|
| 1 | 25  | 2     | +-e         | 0 (1 pair) | 496 | 197056 |
| 2 | 26  | 6     | hexagon     | 2       | 496 | 198550 |
| 3 | 27  | 12    | A3 roots (cuboctahedron) | 4       | 496 | **200540** (ours; published 200044 had only 7 gain-units) |
| 4 | 28  | 24    | D4 roots    | 8       | 496 | 204520 |
| 5 | 29  | 40    | D5 roots    | 13 (+1 spare dir) | 496 | 209496 |
| 6 | 30  | 72    | E6 roots    | 24      | 496 | 220440 |
| 7 | 31  | 126   | E7 roots    | 42      | 496 | 238350 |

Every row is reproduced exactly. **Only dim 27 was suboptimal** and that is the +496
we already claimed.

### Consequences (where the remaining leverage is)

1. **`s` is the whole game.** Raising the maximum class size `s`, counted in **vectors**,
   by 1 gives `+1, +4, +8, +16, +26, +48, +84` points in dims 25...31 respectively; per
   extra **line** it is twice that, `+2, +8, +16, +32, +52, +96, +168`.
   `s = 496` is a *search* record (Kallal et al. 2016 had `s = 488`, PackingStar 2025
   has 496) - **not** a proven optimum.
2. `A = tau_k` is already maximal - no slack.
3. `#triples` is maximal for every `k` except `k = 5` - see section 3.

---

## 2. The class problem (the central open computational question)

**Problem.** Maximise `|S|`, `S` a set of Leech minimal vectors with no two at inner
product 16 (norm-32 scaling) - equivalently `S - S` contains no minimal vector.

Facts established here:

* Optimal `S` may be taken **antipodally closed** (a set of *lines*): a non-antipodal
  independent set decomposes into components that are "balanced" signed graphs, whose
  two sides are cliques at inner product -2 (at most 3 vectors each), so a component of
  `c` lines contributes `c`, whereas an isolated doubled line contributes 2.
  Doubling wins.
* So work with the **98280 lines**. Relations by `|<u,v>|`:
  `32` (self, 1), `16` (4600), `8` (47104), `0` (46575).
* This is a **4-class association scheme**. Computed here:
  * eigenvalues of the conflict graph (relation `16`): `4600, 1000, 76, -20`
  * multiplicities: `1, 299, 17250, 80730` (= dim Harm_0, Harm_2, Harm_4, plus 80730)
  * **Delsarte LP bound = Hoffman ratio bound = 98280*20/4620 = 425.45, so at most 425
    lines (at most 850 vectors).**
* Current best: **248 lines = 496 vectors.** Gap to the LP bound is a factor 1.7.
* The published 248-line classes are **not** vertex-transitive (orthogonality degrees
  range 78...110), so they are search artefacts, not a canonical algebraic object.
  Good sign that 496 is beatable.

### Structure of a class containing a frame

A "frame" = 24 mutually orthogonal Leech minimal lines. The published classes all
contain one. Given a frame `f_1..f_24` (norm 32) and any other class member `g`:
`sum_i (g.f_i)^2 = 1024` with `g.f_i` in `{0,+-8}`, so **exactly 16 of the 24 are
nonzero**. Rescaling `x = (g.f_i)/8`, a class is

    {+-4 e_i} together with vectors in {0,+-1}^24 of weight 16,

with allowed inner products `{0,+-4,+-16}` and forbidden `+-8`.
Two members with the same 16-support are at Hamming distance `h` with
`16 - 2h` in `{0,+-4,+-16}`, i.e. **`h` in `{6,8,10,16}`** - exactly the distance set
of the **Nordstrom-Robinson (16,256,6) code**. (NR is the Gray image of the octacode,
and the Leech lattice is Construction-A4 of a self-dual Z4-code of length 24, so this
is not a coincidence; the frame realises the Z4 picture: `2L` inside `Z^24`,
`2L mod 4` a self-dual Z4-code of length 24 with minimum Euclidean weight 16.)

Frames are **not** all equivalent. Observed types (by number of compatible lines):

| frame | compatible lines | supports x group size | best class found |
|-------|------------------|-----------------------|------------------|
| `4(e_{2i-1} +- e_{2i})`, i=1..12 | 9048 = 24 + 141*64 | 141 x 64 (one octad each; at most 8 lines usable per group, RM(1,3)) | 144 lines |
| random frame (sample) | 11800 = 24 + 720*16 + 8*32 | mixed | - |
| frame inside Cohn's class 0 | 8856 = 24 + 63*128 + 3*256 | 66 supports | 248 (= the record) |

Within one 256-line group a local search reached 34 independent lines (a full NR would
give 128 - the available sign patterns form a coset of a linear code that contains
weight-4 words, so NR is not available there).

### What has been tried (and what it gives)

| method | best |
|--------|------|
| uniform random greedy on the 98280-line graph | 121 lines |
| ILS/(1,2)-swap seeded from the published 248 | no improvement yet |
| MIS inside frame-A candidate graph (9024 vertices) | 144 lines |
| C23-invariant (greedy on orbit graph) | 162 lines |
| C11-invariant (greedy) | 145 lines |
| C3-invariant (greedy) | 129 lines |
| C2-invariant (greedy) | 127 lines |

Group used: `PSL(2,23)` acting on `P^1(F_23)` = the 24 coordinates, preserving the
extended-QR Golay code hence the Leech lattice. Element orders 2,3,4,6,11,12,23.
`2^12` sign changes by Golay codewords are also available.

**Targets:** a C23-invariant class needs 11 orbits (253 lines) to beat 496 vectors;
C11 needs 23 orbits; C3 needs 83 orbits.

---

## 3. Dim 29 has genuine structural slack (+330 available in principle)

The construction does **not** require a *partition* of `W` into triples. In general,
assign to each direction `w` a class `U_w`; define `D_u = {w : u in U_w}`; the
constraints are

* `D_u` is a set of directions pairwise at cos <= -1/2, hence `|D_u| <= 3`;
* each `U_w` is an independent set (`|U_w| <= s`);

and the gain is `sum_u (|D_u| - 1)`. Counting gives

    gain <= (2/3) * |W| * s.

For `k = 1,2,3,4,6,7` the triple *partition* already attains this. For `k = 5`,
`|W| = 40` is not divisible by 3: partition gives `26 s`, the bound is
`floor(80*496/3) = 13226 = 26.67 s`. Explicitly, `D5` has 80 zero-sum triples, each
root in 6 of them; setting `x_t = 82` on all 80 triples gives load `492 <= 496` per
direction and gain `2*6560 = 13120` (+224), and topping up 13 disjoint triples by 4
gives `13224` (+328).

**The obstruction** is that this needs 40 *near-maximum independent sets* `U_w` with a
prescribed intersection pattern (each `U_w` = disjoint union of the 6 blocks on its 6
triples). Hard, but purely combinatorial. Not yet attempted.

---

## 4. Ideas examined and ruled out (keep, so we do not redo them)

* **Extra layer at `beta^2 = 3/4` (heads needing no equator deletion).** Requires a
  direction `z` in `S^{k-1}` at >= 53.7 degrees from every cap direction; the covering
  radius of the 60 degree code `W` is far smaller. Dead for k <= 7.
* **Cap heads off the Leech lattice.** A head `u` costs `|B(u)|` equator deletions
  where `B(u) = {y : <y,u> > sqrt(3/8) = 0.6124}` and pays 3 caps. Minimal vectors give
  `|B| = 1`, gain 2. `u = normalize(u1+u2)` with `u1.u2 = 16` has exactly `|B| = 2`
  (checked: no other minimal vector reaches the threshold) so gain +1 - but such a head
  must satisfy `(u1+u2).v <= 8` for every `v` in the class, and a second-moment estimate
  (`sum_v (w.v)^2 ~ 661|w|^2`, RMS ~ 11 over 496 terms) makes that impossible for a full
  class. Two heads sharing one blocker would gain 5, but both must lie in the shrunken
  Voronoi cell of one minimal vector while being >= 75.5 degrees apart - open, probably
  dead.
* **Level sets of a linear functional** `S = {u : u.z = c}`: needs `z-perp` intersect
  `L` to have minimum >= 6, which by a density comparison against `Lambda_23` forces
  `|z|^2 >= 44944`. Dead.
* **Cosets of a sublattice of minimum >= 6**: same obstruction.
* **`b(u,v) = 1` construction.** With `b(x,y) = x.y mod 2` on `L/2L` (a symplectic
  form), `b = 1` implies inner product odd implies +-1 implies allowed. But a set of
  vectors pairwise non-orthogonal for a symplectic form on `F_2^24` has size at most 25
  (rank of `J+I`). Gives at most 25 lines. Useful only as a sub-structure.
* **Different classes on the three directions of one triple.** Allowed (there is no
  constraint between caps of the same triple), but the gain `sum|U_i| - |union U_i|` is
  maximised by taking them equal. No freedom.
* **Using a non-Leech 24-dim equator, or a d>24 core.** For `d = 32` (ERS config) the
  "quarter-code" `alpha_32` is about 1700, far below the roughly 15600 needed to beat
  the ERS value in dim 33. Leech is the right core for `d <= 31`.

## 5. Dimensions 32-47: the Edel-Rains-Sloane construction

    tau_n  >=  max over n0 in [32..n] of  A(n0, ceil(n0/4))  +  128*A(n,8,8)  +  4*C(n,2)

(3 levels, support sizes `n0 >= 8 >= 2`; the ERS chain condition is `n_i >= 4 n_{i+1}`,
which is why the weight-8 layer forces `n0 >= 32` and why the method starts at dim 32.)
Verified: this reproduces every entry of Cohn's dims 32-47 exactly.

`A(n,8,8)` improved in Aug 2026 (W. Echols, arXiv:2608.13906). Consequences:

| dim | Cohn | new A(n,8,8) | new tau | gain | status |
|-----|------|--------------|---------|------|--------|
| 32 | 345408 | 1667 | 346432 | +1024 | claimed by Echols |
| 33 | 360640 | 1788 | 362048 | +1408 | claimed by Echols |
| 34 | 380868 | 1936 | 381124 | +256  | claimed by Echols |
| 37 | 494312 | 2832 | 496232 | +1920 | claimed by Echols |
| **38** | 566652 | 3025 | **570236** | **+3584** | **unclaimed** (Echols fixed n0=32 and missed that dim 38 uses n0=38) |
| **39** | 755988 | 3324 | **756116** | **+128**  | **unclaimed** |

Level-1 codes used: `A(32,8)=131072`, `A(38,10)=180224`, `A(39,10)=327680`,
`A(40,10)=589824`, `A(44,11)=2^21`, `A(46,12)=2^22`, `A(47,12)=2^23` - none improvable
per current tables.

## 7. SOLVED: the Zinoviev-Litsyn "Y1" codes for A(38,10) and A(39,10)

The level-1 codes that Cohn's dims 38/39 rest on are now **explicit and verified**.

### The theorem (found; primary source read)

V. A. Zinov'ev, S. N. Litsyn, "Об укорочении кодов" (On shortening of codes),
Пробл. передачи информ. 20:1 (1984) 3-11; Engl. transl. Problems Inform.
Transmission 20:1, 1-7. Free scan: `mathnet.ru/eng/ppi1116`.

*Theorem 1 (construction S.l.w; existence, arbitrary linear or nonlinear code).*
Given an (n_z, d_z, N_z) code and integers l, w with `0 < l < n_z`,
`0 <= w < min(l/2, d_z/2)`, then on **any** n_z - l positions there is a code
with

        n = n_z - l,   d = d_z - 2w,   N = ceil( N_z * V(l,w) / 2^l ),
        V(l,w) = sum_{i=0..w} C(l,i)   (volume of a Hamming ball of radius w).

Proof is a pigeonhole/averaging over the 2^l translates on the l deleted
positions.

*Theorem 2 (constructive, linear source).* If Z is a systematic linear
[n_z, k, d_z] code, K an information set, L a subset of K with |L| = l, then

        C = { z restricted to complement of L : z in Z, wt(z|_L) <= w }

has n = n_z - l, d >= d_z - 2w, N = 2^(k-l) * V(l,w) exactly.

**"j" in the LRS key ("Y1 = shortening of codes with j=1") is the ball radius w.**
Confirmed against the paper's own Table 2, which lists the source code
(47, 11, 2^24) = the [47,24,11] QR code with l = 4,5,8,9,10 and w = 1.

### The instantiation for our dimensions

Source Z = **[48,24,12] extended binary quadratic-residue code** (self-dual,
doubly even, weight enum. 1 + 17296 x^12 + 535095 x^16 + ...). Take w = 1, so
V(l,1) = l+1, and L any l coordinates (always inside an information set for
l <= 11, because d(Z^perp) = d(Z) = 12):

        A(48-l, 10)  >=  (l+1) * 2^(24-l).

| l | n = 48-l | (l+1)2^(24-l) | LRS d=9 row |
|---|----------|---------------|-------------|
| 4 | 44 | 5*2^20 = 5242880 | A(43,9) >= 5*2^20 Y1 [zino84a] |
| 5 | 43 | 6*2^19 = 3145728 | A(42,9) >= 3*2^20 Y1 |
| 8 | 40 | 9*2^16 = 589824  | A(39,9) >= 9*2^16 Y1 |
| 9 | 39 | 10*2^15 = 327680 | A(38,9) >= 5*2^16 Y1 |
|10 | 38 | 11*2^14 = 180224 | A(37,9) >= 11*2^14 Y1 |
|11 | 37 | 12*2^13 = 98304  | A(36,9) >= 3*2^15 |

Why the chain has holes at n = 41, 42: l = 7, 6 give 2^20 and 7*2^18, both
beaten by the [42,21,10] extended QR code (2^21).

### Files (all verified, see section 6 additions)

* `research/lib/qr48.py` - the [48,24,12] extended QR code from scratch
  (cyclic QR(47) idempotent + parity bit); `qr48_selftest()` recomputes the
  dimension, self-duality, double-evenness and the full weight distribution.
* `research/exp/zl_shorten.py` - the S.l.w construction + exhaustive check.
* `research/exp/zl_search.py` - max-clique search for a **larger** admissible
  coset set A than the ball of radius 1. **Result: |A| = l+1 is optimal** for
  the [48,24,12] source (exact max-clique, 7 different L's per l, l = 8,9,10).
  So 180224 / 327680 / 589824 cannot be improved this way.
* `research/exp/zl_sweep.py` - applies the S.l.w bound to **every** entry of the
  LRS table; confirms 180224 (n=38) and 327680 (n=39) are the best the method
  yields from any known source code.
* `research/data/codes/zl_qr48_n{38,39,40,43,44}_*.txt.gz` - explicit codeword
  lists (hex, one per line, with a self-contained header).
* `research/data/codes/verify_zl.py` - **independent** verifier: reads only the
  hex list, rediscovers the coset structure, and proves the exact minimum
  distance two ways (coset-difference enumeration; optional full brute force).

Best known **linear** comparison (codetables.de): [38,17,10] and [39,18,10] are
optimal for their k, i.e. 131072 and 262144. The ZL codes beat them by 11/8 and
10/8 respectively.

## 8. NEW (this session): the cap construction at large k, and where it can win

The construction of section 1 was only ever used with `k <= 7`. Written for a general base
dimension `d` and a general `k` it reads

    tau(d+k) >= tau_d + 4 * cL(T_k) + A_k

where, in the Leech case `d = 24`:
* `T_k` = number of pairwise disjoint zero-sum triples available in the best 60-degree
  code in `R^k` (each triple = 3 unit vectors pairwise at cos -1/2, summing to 0);
* `cL(T)` = the largest number of Leech minimal-vector LINES that can be written as a
  union of `T` pairwise disjoint independent sets (a partial `T`-colouring of the
  98280-vertex conflict graph);
* `A_k <= tau_k` is the axis layer.

Check: `k = 7`, `T = 42`, `cL = 42*248 = 10416`, gives `196560 + 41664 + 126 = 238350` -
the published dim-31 record, exactly.

### Hard caps

`cL <= 98280`, so `tau(24+k) <= 3*196560 + tau_k = 589680 + tau_k`. Comparing with the
current records this can only ever win in dims 36, 37, 38 (589680 beats 484568, 496232,
570236 but loses to 756116 in dim 39 and to everything above).

`T_k` for the best known `k`-dimensional configurations (measured here):

| k | tau_k | zero-sum triples | max disjoint (found) | possible max |
|---|-------|------------------|----------------------|--------------|
| 9 | 306 | 1344 | 89 | 102 |
| 12 | 841 | **0** (Cohn's config has none) | 0 | 280 |
| 13 | 1154 | 3800 | 293 | 384 |
| 14 | 1932 | 8288 | **623 and rising** | 644 |
| 15 | 2564 | 6176 | 312 | 854 |
| 16 | 4320 | 201600 | 1368 | 1440 |

So dim 36 is dead (`T <= 280` forces average class size 256 > 248 = the maximum known),
dim 37 needs 195 lines per class over 384 classes (very hard), and **dim 38 needs
`cL >= 93419` lines (95.05%) out of 98280 using `T ~ 623` classes, i.e. an average of
150 lines per class.**

### The colouring problem

`chi_f = 98280/248 = 396.3`, so fractionally 397 colours suffice - there is no
information-theoretic obstruction to 623.

Measured colouring quality on the 98280-line graph:

| method | class size | full cover at |
|--------|-----------|---------------|
| uniform random greedy | 113 | T = 948 |
| best-of-16 greedy | 137 falling to 115 | T = 800 (95.60% at T=750) |
| best-of-64 greedy | 139 | - |

best-of-16 sequential covers only **84.01%** at T = 644. The gap to 95.05% is about 14%
in average class size. A proper "maximum induced K-colourable subgraph" local search is
the remaining lever.

### The algebraic colouring (clean, but too fine)

If `M` is a sublattice of `Lambda` with **minimum norm >= 6**, then two minimal vectors in
the same coset of `M` differ by an element of `M`, which is not a minimal vector - so
**every coset of `M` is automatically a valid class**, and the cosets give a proper
colouring with `[Lambda:M]` colours, for free.

Taking `2*Lambda <= M`, this becomes: find a large subspace `V` of `Lambda/2Lambda = F_2^24`
containing none of the 98280 minimal-vector cosets; then `[Lambda:M] = 2^(24-dim V)`.

* density bound: `delta(M) = 1.5^12 / q <= 1` forces `q = [Lambda:M] >= 130`;
* LP bound: each class has at most 850 vectors, and the classes average `196560/q`, so
  `q >= 232`. Hence `dim V <= 16`, and `q = 256` is the best possible power of two.
* found computationally: `dim V = 13` (q = 2048, classes of 96 vectors); `dim V = 12`
  reproduces the classical partition `196560 = 4095 x 48` into coordinate frames.
* **no two cosets of a maximal such `M` can ever be merged** (merging two cosets is
  exactly extending `V` by their difference), so this route is locally maximal and,
  at `q = 2048`, gives far too many colours.

### Status

Secured this session: dims 38 and 39 (section 7). The `589680 + tau_k` route for dim 38
is open and needs (a) `T = 644` disjoint triples in a 1932-point 60-degree code in `R^14`
(623 found so far by local search) and (b) a 95% partial 623-colouring.

---

## 9. Rigidity of the 496-vector class (why local search fails)

For each of the five published classes, in the VECTOR graph (196560 vertices, conflict =
inner product exactly +16):

* the minimum number of conflicts of an outside vector with the class is **4** (only 80
  vectors attain it), so no (1,2)-, (2,3)- or (3,4)-swap exists;
* for every outside vector `v`, the set of vectors whose whole conflict set with the class
  lies inside `N(v)` is exactly `N(v) union {v}` - so removing `N(v)` frees nothing new.
  Checked for all 95840 vectors with at most 11 conflicts.

Structure of a class (248 lines):
* it is **124 orthogonal pairs**; the 124 pair-differences take only 14 distinct values in
  `Lambda/2Lambda` and span a **4-dimensional totally isotropic subspace `R`** whose 15
  non-zero elements are all norm-8 cosets;
* hence the class lies inside `X_R = {lines with b(.,R) = 0}`, which has **6120** of the
  98280 lines (the other 15 cosets have 6144 each) - a 16-fold reduction of the search
  space;
* `X_R` splits into **2925 fibres** (cosets of `R`): 2880 of size 2 and 45 of size 8, and
  every fibre is internally conflict-free. The published class uses 124 fibres of size 2
  and none of size 8.
* random isotropic 4-spaces give a completely different (useless) profile: 6376 lines,
  mostly fibres of size 1, best class about 102 lines.

Searches run: seeded ILS in `X_R` (no improvement on 248); weighted fibre MIS in `X_R`
(216 lines); tabu at fixed size 249 (stuck at 4 conflicting pairs); C23/C11/C3/C2-invariant
orbit MIS (max 167 lines).

The **non-antipodal** option is genuinely open (both Kallal et al. and PackingStar assumed
antipodal closure) - the earlier "doubling always wins" argument in section 2 is WRONG:
the single-line components only need each *clique* to have size at most 3, not each
component. The vector-graph LP bound is the same 850, and the LP optimum is attained by an
antipodal distribution, but that does not settle it.

## 10. RESULT: dimension 38, tau_38 >= 591588 (verified)

> **SUPERSEDED by section 99 (2026-08-27): the claim is 591 612.** Everything below is
> right except the axis layer, which reads 1908 here and is 1932 now -- the rotation was
> found by descent on SO(14) rather than by sampling, and the construction is at its
> ceiling 3*tau(24) + tau(14).

Package: `research/dim38-leech/`. Improves Cohn's published 566652 by **+24936**.

The cap construction of section 1, run at k = 14 instead of k <= 7. Three things had to
line up, and all three worked:

1. **A perfect triple partition.** Cohn's 1932-point dimension-14 kissing configuration
   (all norms 8, max off-diagonal inner product 4, so a genuine 60-degree code) splits into
   **644 = 1932/3 disjoint zero-sum triples**. Found by ILS on the 3-uniform hypergraph of
   its 8288 zero-sum triples; naive greedy stalls at 546, ILS with (2,3)-swaps reaches 644.
2. **A complete class partition.** All **98280 Leech lines partition into 644 disjoint
   classes** with no class containing a pair at cos = 1/2. So the equator empties out and
   the cap layer alone has 6 * 98280 = 589680 points = the exact ceiling 3*tau_24.
3. **A non-empty axis layer anyway.** An axis point only needs 30 degrees from every cap
   direction, and the covering radius of the 1932-point code is **54.7 degrees**. A rotated
   copy of the same code loses only 24 points to the 30-degree caps: **+1908**.
   The rotation is a product of six integer Householder reflections, hence rational, so the
   check `((Qw_i).w_j)^2 <= 48 D^2` is exact integer arithmetic.

Verified two independent ways: `scripts/verify.py` (exact, case analysis, every class
checked exhaustively) and `scripts/spotcheck.py` (materialises ~10000 actual 38-dimensional
unit vectors, over-sampling every pair type, and finds max inner product exactly 0.5).

### The extraction that produced the 644-class partition

The key is the **arena**: a maximum class lies inside `X_R` for a 4-dimensional totally
isotropic `R` (section 9), only 6120 of the 98280 lines. Local search there returns
230-244 lines; the same search on the whole graph returns 152, and a random greedy 113.

| method | class size | full cover at |
|--------|-----------|---------------|
| uniform random greedy | 113 | T = 948 |
| best-of-16 greedy | 137 | T = 800 |
| ILS on the full graph | 152 | ~T = 700 |
| **ILS inside an arena, falling back to global** | **230 falling to 140** | **T = 644** |

Arenas are transported by the Golay sign-change automorphisms (4096 of them) and, later,
by `M24` as well (`research/lib/m24.py` finds Golay-code automorphisms instantly by
backtracking on the Steiner system S(5,8,24)).

**Important negative:** a random 4-dimensional isotropic `R` with the *same* invariant
|X_R| = 6120 gives classes of only ~121 lines. The good arenas are a genuine Co_0-orbit,
not just anything with the right size.

### Why only dimension 38

`total = tau_d + 4 c_L + A` with `c_L <= min(tau_d/2, T_k * alpha_d)`. For d = 24 the
ceiling is `3 * 196560 = 589680` plus the axis layer.

| dim | k | T_k | c_L reached | total | record | verdict |
|-----|---|-----|-------------|-------|--------|---------|
| 32 | 8 | 80 | 18017 | 268865 | 346432 | too few triples |
| 33 | 9 | 102 | 22608 | 287294 | 362048 | too few triples |
| 34 | 10 | 170 | 35628 | 339577 | 381124 | too few triples |
| 35 | 11 | 201 | 41001 | 361162 | 409548 | too few triples |
| 36 | 12 | 280 | 53175 | 410093 | 484568 | too few triples |
| 37 | 13 | ~360 | 67591 | ~468000 | 496232 | needs c_L >= 74632, i.e. +10% |
| **38** | **14** | **644** | **98280** | **591588** | 570236 | **WIN** |
| 39 | 15 | 854 | 98280 | ~592200 | 756116 | ceiling too low |
| 40+ | | | | <= 594000 | 1064368 | ceiling too low |

Other bases are useless: `alpha_d` for the dimension-16 configuration is 108 vectors, for
dimension 20 it is 718, and for dimension 23 it is at most 496 (the dimension-23
configuration is a subset of the Leech shell, so its quarter-code cannot beat the Leech
one). `tau_24 = 196560` is what makes the family work at all.

### Dimension 37 is the one near miss

It needs 384 disjoint classes covering 74632 of the 98280 lines (194 per class); the best
extraction gives 67591 (176 per class), and a max-induced-384-colourable-subgraph local
search seeded from it does not move. The class-size profile of the 644-class partition is
2 classes >= 240, 25 >= 230, 115 >= 200, 171 >= 180 -- it thins out much too fast.

## 11. Small dimensions (9-16): the layered framework, and why they are hard

### The framework that holds every small-dimension record

Every record configuration in dims 10-15 is a **layered (multi-shell) spherical code**.
Split `R^{d+1} = R^d + R` and lay the points on parallel levels `h` (the last coordinate):

    point = (sqrt(1-h^2) * u, h),   u in S^{d-1}

* within level `h`:      `<u,u'> <= (1/2 - h^2)/(1 - h^2)`
* between `h` and `h'`:  `<u,u'> <= (1/2 - h h')/sqrt((1-h^2)(1-h'^2))`

Total = sum of the level sizes. Levels found in the record configurations (verified by
computing the height spectrum along the best axis):

| dim | levels | sizes | total |
|-----|--------|-------|-------|
| 10 | 0, +-1/4, +-1/2, +-1 | 216, 64, 82, 1 | 510 |
| 11 | 0, +-1/2, +-1 | 386, 108, 1 | 604 |
| 13 | 0, +-1/4, +-sqrt3/4, +-1/2, +-1 | 464, 232, 8, 104, 1 | 1154 |
| 12 | **no layering** (782 distinct heights) | - | 841 |

The `+-1/2` levels can **share** their code (the two points `(sqrt3/2 u, +-1/2)` are at inner
product exactly 1/2), which is why that level is worth **2** per head. Sharing requires
`|h| >= 1/2`, and `h = 1/2` maximises the within-level threshold, so `+-1/2` is forced.

### The m-parameter family (new here)

More generally, split `R^{a+b}` and put caps at level `alpha^2 = (m+1)/(2m)`. Then

* head condition: `cos <= 1/(m+1)`
* directions: partitioned into regular simplices of `m` points (`cos = -1/(m-1)`)
* axis threshold: `cos <= 1/(2 beta)`, i.e. **no constraint at all when m = 2**

    total = tau_a + (m-1) * N_m * s_m + A_b,     s_m = max head set with cos <= 1/(m+1)

so the coefficient is `(m-1)/m` and the head set shrinks with `m`. Measured `s_m`:

| base a | s_2 (cos<=1/3) | s_3 (cos<=1/4) | s_4 (cos<=1/5) | best m |
|--------|----------------|----------------|----------------|--------|
| 8  | 16  | 16  | 16 | 3 |
| 9  | 24  | 24  | 18 | 3 |
| 10 | **80** | 39  | 18 | **2** |
| 11 | 48  | 48  | 22 | 3 |
| 24 (Leech) | 496 | 496 | 48 | **3** |

For the Leech lattice the cosine spectrum is `{0,+-1/4,+-1/2,+-1}`, so `s_2 = s_3` and
`m = 3` wins - which is why the classical dims 25-31 construction uses `m = 3`. For generic
configurations `s_2` can be much larger and `m = 2` wins. **This is a genuine extra degree
of freedom that the dim-25-31 literature never needed.**

### Dim 11 in detail (record 604, the weakest record relative to its neighbours)

`tau_{d+1}/tau_d` is 1.67, **1.18**, 1.39, 1.37, 1.67, 1.33, 1.68 for d = 9..15 - dim 11 is
by far the flattest step, which is why it looks attackable.

Structure (decoded here): the 604 points all have norm 4 and split as `R^8 + R^3` into four
shells by `(|x|^2, |w|^2)`:

    (4,0): 112 points   (3,1): 384   (2,2): 96   (0,4): 12  (a cuboctahedron)

To reproduce that last layer from `rec11_exact.npz` (it is not stored as plain vectors, and
reading the arrays raw shows squared norms 0, 18, 36, which is why it looks wrong at first):
the file holds `A`, `B` (both 604x11 int8) and `den = 3`, and the point is `(A + B*sqrt2)/3`.
Every row has `A.B = 0` and `|A|^2 + 2|B|^2 = 36`, so every squared norm is exactly 4. Split
the coordinates 8 + 3; the twelve rows whose `R^8` part vanishes are the layer, and their
pairwise cosines come out `-1` (6 pairs), `-1/2` (24), `0` (12), `+1/2` (24) with no
irrational cross terms -- the A_3 root system. An icosahedron would give `+-1/sqrt5` and no
pair at `-1/2` at all.

and the 60-degree condition is exactly `x.x' + w.w' <= 2`. The `R^8` parts are **integral**,
which is what makes most cross-shell conditions automatic (`|x.y| <= sqrt(4*2) = 2.83 < 3`).

Equivalently, in the level picture it is `386 + 2*108 + 2` with

* `S_0`  : 386 points, a 60-degree code in `R^10` (max cos exactly 1/2)
* `S_b`  : 108 points, an `arccos(1/3)`-code in `R^10` (max cos exactly 1/3), shared by `+-1/2`
* cross  : max cos exactly `1/sqrt3` -- **every constraint is tight**

### What was ruled out for dim 11

* **Adding any point:** the covering radius of the 604-configuration has `cos = 0.5802 > 1/2`,
  so it is maximal. Same for dims 9 (0.5775), 10 (0.7119), 12 (0.6237), 13 (0.5984).
* **Adding a head:** the 108-point head code is maximal too (its covering radius has
  `cos = 0.448 > 1/3`). The best new head found anywhere has `|B_0| = 3` equator conflicts
  and `|B_b| = 3` head conflicts, i.e. net **-7**.
* **Off-configuration heads:** a head not in the equator costs `|B(u)|` deletions and pays
  `m-1`. The `|B| = 1` cell of a configuration point has angular radius only `5.3` degrees
  (the nearest neighbour sits at 60 degrees and the threshold is 54.7), so two heads can
  never share a cell. Dead for every dense configuration, Leech included.
* **Layering the record configuration one dimension up:** `tau(d+1) >= n_d + 2 s + 2` with
  `s` the third-code of the record configuration. Measured: `s/n` is only 5-8% everywhere
  (dim 12: 54/841, dim 13: 61/1154, dim 15: 156/2564), while one needs 18%, 34%, 34%.
  Never competitive.
* **Naive continuous optimisation.** L-BFGS on the smooth penalty, from random starts,
  cannot even reproduce the *known* feasible pair `(386,108)` - it stalls at ~9900 violating
  pairs. These configurations are far too structured for blind descent. (Consistent with
  EinsteinArena: dim-11 `n = 605` and dim-12 `n = 842` have been open targets since spring
  2026, with many agents attacking them and best scores still 1.71 and 0.55, not 0.)

### The relevant LP ceilings (computed here)

| dim | cos <= 1/2 (60 deg) | cos <= 1/3 (70.53 deg) | cos <= 1/sqrt3 (54.74 deg) |
|-----|--------------------|------------------------|----------------------------|
| 9  | -   | **108.0** | 766 |
| 10 | 596 | **146.0** | 1337 |
| 11 | 915 | -   | -   |
| 12 | 1416| 257 | 3885 |

So for dim 11 the head code could in principle reach 146 rather than 108; each extra head
is worth **+2** and costs about 1.15 equator points, so a 120-point head code with a
370-point equator would give `370 + 240 + 2 = 612`. **Building an `arccos(1/3)`-code in
`R^10` with more than 108 points is the single clearest open lever for dim 11.**
Integer attempts so far: norm-3 vectors in `Z^10` give 104, norm-6 give 92.

### Landscape check (2020-2026)

Nothing improves dims 9, 13, 15, 16 since 2020. Dim 9 is capped at 272 for *lattices*
(Dutour Sikiric-van Woerden 2026). Dims 10, 11, 14 are Ganzhinov (510 / 592 / 1932);
dim 11 was pushed 592 -> 604 on EinsteinArena in April 2026; dim 12 = 841 is Takhanov et al.
(June 2026). AlphaEvolve only ever reached 593 in dim 11 and never tried dims 12-16.


---

## 12. The Zinoviev-Ericson "extension" scheme, decoded (dims 9-16)

Every structured small-dimension record is an instance of one of three level schemes. The
layering inequality (section 11) with the actual codes:

| scheme | levels h | within-level cos | cross-level cos |
|--------|----------|------------------|-----------------|
| A | 0, +-1/2, +-1 | 1/2 ; **1/3** | equator-cap **1/sqrt3 = 0.577350** |
| B | 0, +-1/4, +-1/2, +-1 | 1/2 ; **7/15 = 0.46667** ; 1/3 | 0-1/4 0.516398 ; 0-1/2 0.577350 ; 1/4-1/2 0.447214 ; 1/4 to -1/4 0.6 ; 1/4 to -1/2 0.745356 |
| C | 0, +-1/(2 sqrt2), +-1/sqrt2 | 1/2 ; **3/7 = 0.42857** ; **0** | 0-mid 0.534522 ; 0-out 0.707107 ; mid-out 0.377964 |

Which record uses which, with the exact layer sizes (all verified against Cohn's data):

| dim | scheme | layers | total |
|-----|--------|--------|-------|
| 9  | A | 176 + 2*64  + 2      | 306 |
| 11 | A | 386 + 2*108 + 2      | 604 |
| 13 | A | 816 + 2*168 + 2      | 1154 |
| 10 | B | 216 + 2*64 + 2*82 + 2| 510 |
| 15 | B | 1114 + 2*512 + 2*212 + 2 | 2564 |
| 14 | C | 984 + 2*448 + 2*26   | 1932 |
| 16 | C | 2340 + 2*960 + 2*30  | 4320 |
| 12 | - | numerical, no layering | 841 |

**Every within-level constraint is met with exact equality** in all seven.

Dimension 13 = 1154 in full (Zinoviev-Litsyn-Ericson 1999, Theorem 1, and it is their
*only* new result together with tau_14 >= 1606):

* equator, radius 4: all `(+-2^4, 0^8)` on an optimal `A(12,4,4) = 51` constant-weight
  code -> `16 * 51 = 816`
* caps at h = +-1/2, radius `2 sqrt3`: the 24 cross-polytope points `+-2 sqrt3 e_i`, plus
  `(+-1^12)` on an optimal `A(12,4) = 144` code -> `168` per cap, the same set on both
* two poles.

Both code inputs are provably optimal (`A(12,4,4)=51`, `A(12,4)=144`). The whole gain over
the older `P_13a = 1130` is that the 24 axis directions are worth 48 as caps and only 24 on
the equator: `1154 - 1130 = 24` exactly.

A useful closed form: the `+-1/2` level needs the equator's "rho" to be at least 4/3, and
the `phi(A(n-1,4))` construction delivers that only when `16/(n-1) >= 4/3`, i.e. **n <= 13**.
Dimension 13 is the last dimension where scheme A works at all - which is why dim 14 needs
scheme C.

### Improvement leads tested and killed

* **dim 13 with the 841-point record as equator.** Would give `841 + 2*168 + 2 = 1179`.
  Dead: the largest cosine a new unit vector can have against the 841-point configuration is
  **0.605882 > 1/sqrt3**, so *no* admissible cap point exists at all. Against `P_12a` (840
  points) the same quantity is exactly `0.577350269 = 1/sqrt3` - which is precisely how the
  144-point cap code arises in the first place. The 841st sphere actively poisons the
  equator for this construction.
* **Trading the 24 axis vectors.** Putting `j` of them on the equator instead of the caps
  gives `1154 - j`; `j = 0` is optimal.
* **Enlarging any layer.** All eight configurations are saturated: the minimum achievable
  max-cosine for a new point is 0.577350 (d9), 0.670820 (d10), 0.577350 (d11), 0.612002
  (d12), 0.561487 (d13), 0.534522 (d14), 0.534522 (d15), 0.577350 (d16) - all above 1/2.
* **dim 12 -> 842 by re-seating the loose points.** The 28 points with no contact above
  cos 0.4999 are not true rattlers (they all have contacts at 0.4990), and fixing the other
  813 and re-optimising leaves room for exactly 28, not 29 (best energy 9.5e-2 at K = 29
  versus 0 at K = 28).

### Where the arccos(1/3) codes stand

The cap code of scheme A is an `arccos(1/3)`-code, and **the minimal vectors of any integral
lattice with minimum 3 form one** (since `|u-v|^2 >= 3` forces `u.v <= 1 = mu/3`). Sizes:

| dim | best known | LP bound | integer-shell search here (norm 3 in Z^n) |
|-----|-----------|----------|-------------------------------------------|
| 9  | -   | 108 | - |
| 10 | **108** (in the dim-11 record) | **146** | 104 |
| 12 | **168** (in the dim-13 record) | 257 | 160 |

So the norm-3 shell of `Z^n` gets within 4-5% of the record codes but never beats them.
**Building an `arccos(1/3)`-code in `R^10` with more than 108 points remains the single
cleanest open lever for dimension 11**: each extra head is worth +2 and costs about 1.15
equator points, so 120 heads with a 370-point equator would already give 612 > 604.

## 13. The cap level `t` is a free parameter (RESULT: dim 25 = 197058)

Every published record in dims 25-31 uses caps `(sqrt(t) u, sqrt(1-t) w)` with `t = 2/3`.
That value is **not** intrinsic. Write `t = alpha^2`. The four constraints are

| constraint | inequality | why |
|---|---|---|
| head class stays 496 | `1 - 1/(2t) >= 1/4`, i.e. `t >= 2/3` | same-direction caps need `cos(u,u') <= 1-1/(2t)` |
| a head serves `m` directions | `t <= (m+1)/(2m)` | directions pairwise at `cos <= -1/(m-1)` |
| axis layer admissible | `sqrt(1-t) * max<a,w> <= 1/2` | cap-axis |
| equator survives | `sqrt(t)/2 <= 1/2` | always true |

For `k >= 2` triples exist, `m = 3` forces `t <= 2/3`, hence `t = 2/3` exactly, and the axis
layer already attains its ceiling `tau_k`. **Dimension 25 is the unique exception**: the
second block is `R^1`, there is no triple, `m = 2`, so `t` is free in `[2/3, 3/4]`. At
`t = 3/4` the head condition relaxes to `cos <= 1/3` (identical to `cos <= 1/4` for Leech
inner products, so the class is still 496) and the cap-pole product falls from
`sqrt(1/3) = 0.5774` to exactly `1/2`, admitting the two poles that everybody discards:

    196064 + 992 + 2 = 197058   vs the published 197056.

Verified exactly in `research/dim25/verify.py`. The three constraints `t >= 2/3`,
`t <= 3/4`, `t >= 3/4` meet in the single point `t = 3/4`.

## 14. Second-shell cap heads are FREE but cost too much (ruled out, with proof)

**Theorem.** If `v` is a Leech vector of norm 6 (norm 48 in the norm-32 scaling) and `y` is
any minimal vector, then `v.y <= 3`, because `|v-y|^2 = 10 - 2 v.y >= 4`. Hence
`cos(v,y) <= 3/(2 sqrt6) = sqrt(3/8)` -- **exactly** the cap-head threshold at `t = 2/3`
(the equator-cap condition is `<y,u> <= 1/(2 sqrt t) = sqrt(3/8)`).

So a norm-6 direction may be used as a cap head **without deleting anything from the
equator**: it is worth 3 caps instead of the 2 net that a minimal-vector head is worth.
More generally a norm-`N` head is free exactly at level `t = 4/N`; `N = 6` gives `t = 2/3`,
`N = 8` gives `t = 1/2`.

It still loses, for two independent reasons, both measured here:

1. **The class condition tightens.** Norm-6 vectors have `cos = c/6` with `c <= 4`, so
   `cos <= 1/4` becomes `c <= 1`, i.e. `cos <= 1/6`. The largest such set found is **58**
   directions, against 248 lines = 496 vectors for minimal-vector heads; `3 x 58 = 174`
   versus `2 x 496 = 992`.
2. **Cross-triple incompatibility.** For every norm-6 vector `v`, exactly **552** minimal
   vectors satisfy `v.y = 3` (the maximum). Those 552 may not be heads on any other triple
   (they need `v.y <= 2`), so each free head costs up to 552 minimal-vector heads. The
   inner-product distribution of a norm-48 vector against the 196560 minimal vectors is the
   same for every one of them: `(+-24: 552, +-16: 11178, +-8: 48600, 0: 75900)`.

Also useful: the covering radius of the Leech minimal-vector spherical code is
`cos = 0.614863` (52.06 degrees) against the free-head threshold `sqrt(3/8) = 0.612372`
(52.24 degrees) -- so **no** off-lattice free head exists, by a margin of only 0.0025. The
ceiling `3 tau_24 = 589680` of the cap layer therefore cannot be exceeded.

## 15. The 16-fibre partition of the Leech lines (the tool for dimension 37)

Let `R` be the 4-dimensional totally isotropic subspace of `Lambda/2Lambda` attached to a
maximum class (section 9): the 248 lines of the class split into 124 pairs with equal
`b`-signature, and the 124 sums `u+v` all have **norm 64** (frame type) and span `R`. Then
`l -> (b(l,r_1),...,b(l,r_4))` maps the 98280 lines onto `F_2^4` with fibre sizes

    zero fibre 6120  (this is the arena X_R)   +   fifteen fibres of 6144   =   98280.

**Classes found in different fibres are automatically disjoint.** This is the right way to
manufacture *many* disjoint classes, which is what the cap construction needs whenever the
number of triples is large.

Measured inside the arena (fibre 0), extracting classes greedily until it is exhausted:

    46 classes cover all 6120 lines; the first 24 cover 5156 lines, mean 214.8
    (the first 15 are all 226-240)

whereas the same greedy on the *whole* line set averages only 176 over its first 384
classes. Note `16 x 24 = 384` and `16 x 6120 = 97920 ~ 98280`: the arithmetic lines up.

Arenas for different `R` are never disjoint (`X_R n X_R' = X_{R+R'}`, always non-empty), so
the fibres of one `R` -- not a family of arenas -- are the object to use.

## 16. Dimension 37 is the reachable target of the cap construction

For `dim = 24+k` with a direction code `W` of `n` points carrying `T` disjoint zero-sum
triples and `P` disjoint pairs at `cos <= -1/2` from the leftovers,

    N = 196560 + 4 c_tri + 2 c_pair + A,      c_tri, c_pair = lines in triple/pair classes

(a head on a triple is worth 3 caps minus 1 deletion per vector = 4 per line; on a pair,
2 caps minus 1 deletion = 2 per line). With `3T + 2P = n` this is `196560 + (n+T) s + A`
for classes of common size `s`, subject to `((n-T)/2) s <= 98280`.

Published records versus what this construction needs (`s` = mean class size in lines,
maximum possible 248):

| dim | k | n = tau_k | max T | published | mean class size needed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 34 | 10 | 510 | 170 | 380868 | 270 -- impossible |
| 35 | 11 | 604 | 201 | 409548 | 269 -- impossible |
| 36 | 12 | 841 | 280 | 484568 | 256 -- impossible |
| **37** | **13** | **1154** | **363 (LP bound; 357 found)** | **496232** (Brouwer, not Cohn's stale 494312) | **~210 -- see below** |
| 38 | 14 | 1932 | 644 | 566652 | 169 -- done, 591588 |

So dimension 37 is the only remaining dimension where this family can win, and it needs
about **385 disjoint classes averaging 193 lines** (the arena measurement above gives
214.8 over 24, so the target is not far). The ceiling, at the maximum `s = 248`, is

    196560 + 4(384 x 248) + 2(248) + A  =  579124        (published 494312)

Zero-sum triples in the published direction codes (robust float test, tolerance 1e-8):

| dim | points | pairs at cos -1/2 | zero-sum triples | greedy disjoint T | max T |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 10 | 510 | 20670 | 2570 | 153 | 170 |
| 11 | 604 | 19704 | 1896 | 78 | 201 |
| 12 | 841 | 6 | 0 | 0 | 280 |
| 13 | 1154 | 59640 | 3800 | 356 (ILS) | 384 |
| 14 | 1932 | 159264 | 8288 | 644 (perfect) | 644 |

Dimension 12's record configuration has essentially **no** `cos = -1/2` pairs at all, so it
is useless as a direction code -- a good reason the construction was never tried at `k=12`.

## 17. The cap construction with a NON-Leech base (swept; only the Leech works)

Nothing in the construction uses the Leech lattice. For **any** 60-degree code `Y` in `R^m`
and any `k`,

    N(m+k) = |Y| + 4c + tau_k      (k >= 2, triples, level t = 2/3)
    N(m+1) = |Y| + 2c + 2          (k  = 1, antipodal pair, level t = 3/4, section 13)

with `c` = number of *lines* of `Y` used as heads, split into `T_k` disjoint classes. Each
pair type reduces exactly as before; in particular equator-cap needs `cos <= sqrt(3/8)`,
which every 60-degree code satisfies automatically. So the construction is completely
general. Sweeping every (base, k) pair against the record table, the only cases not already
killed by the ceiling `tau(m+k) < 3 tau_m + tau_k` need these class sizes:

| target | base | k | T_k | required class (lines) | base lines | needed fraction |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 25-31 | 24 | 1-7 | 1-42 | **248** | 98280 | 0.25% -- **achieved** |
| 37 | 24 | 13 | 357 | 210 (mean) | 98280 | see section 16 |
| 33 | 32 | 1 | 1 | 7807 | 172704 | 4.5% |
| 34 | 32 | 2 | 2 | 4432 | 172704 | 2.6% |
| 45 | 44 | 1 | 1 | 49303 | 1474276 | 3.3% |

**All the non-Leech ones fail, for a structural reason worth recording.** In an
Edel-Rains-Sloane code the three levels are

* level 1: `+-1` vectors from a distance-8 binary code; two of them are at `cos = 1/2` iff
  their Hamming distance is 8 or 24, so a class inside level 1 is a subcode with all
  distances in `{12,16,20,28,32}`, hence at most `A(32,12) = 256` vectors = **128 lines**;
* level 2: `+-2` on a weight-8 support. Two vectors on the *same* support are at `cos = 1/2`
  iff their signs differ in 2 or 6 places, so the sign patterns must be a length-8 code with
  distances in `{4,8}` -- the extended Hamming `[8,4,4]`, exactly 16 patterns = **8 lines
  per support**. Two vectors on *different* supports conflict only when the supports meet in
  exactly 4 points, so the supports used must pairwise meet in `<= 3`, i.e. form a constant
  weight code of distance 10. Hence level 2 contributes at most `8 * A(n,10,8)`, and the
  Johnson bound gives `A(32,10,8) <= 440`, `A(36,10,8) <= 765`, `A(44,10,8) <= 1892`;
* level 3: `+-4` on a weight-2 support -- at most a perfect matching, `n/2` supports, 2 lines
  each, and these never conflict with level 1.

So the biggest class an ERS code can have is about `8 A(n,10,8) + 128 + n`, i.e. roughly
**3500 lines for n = 32-36** and **15000 for n = 44** -- a factor 2 to 3 short of what is
needed every time. The Leech lattice is the only base where the record ratio
`tau_{m+1}/tau_m` is small enough (1.0025) for a class to bridge it, which is precisely
because `tau_24 = 196560` is such an outlier.

**Corollary.** The cap construction is a Leech-neighbourhood tool. It can only ever win in
dimensions 25-31 (where it holds the records), 37 and 38 (where it needs a large family of
disjoint classes), and nowhere else in 1-47.

## 18. Dimension 37: measured, and it falls about 4% short

Full run of the machinery of sections 15-16 (2026-08-20):

* **Triples.** The 1154-point dimension-13 code has 3800 zero-sum triples; every direction
  lies in at least one and the hypergraph is connected, so nothing structural blocks a
  perfect packing, but the **LP relaxation of the set packing gives T <= 363.59**, so
  `T <= 363` -- not the naive 384. Iterated local search reaches **T = 357**; HiGHS reaches
  351 with a 3.6% gap after 25 minutes. Leftover directions pair up antipodally
  (the code is antipodally symmetric, 577 antipodal pairs): `P = 41`.
* **Classes.** Best disjoint-class cover found (arena pool = 4096 sign changes x 121 Golay
  automorphisms, strong per-arena search): **400 classes, 71894 lines, mean 179.7**. The
  first 40 classes average 238, the first 120 average 227, the first 357 average 188. A
  simultaneous "maximum k-colourable subgraph" local search (insertions plus (1,1) swaps
  that free a line and re-place it, `research/exp/multiclass.py`) adds only about 1.5%.
* **Result.**

      N = 196560 + 4*67122 + 2*4568 + 1152 = 475336      vs the record 496232 (-20896)

  To win, the top 357 classes would have to average **209 lines instead of 188** -- an 11%
  improvement in a packing problem where the maximum single class is 248 and the greedy
  profile is already within 4% of the best simultaneous optimisation found. Not reachable
  by search; it would need a better *class* (section 2), not a better cover.

Note the record for dimension 37 is **496232** (Brouwer, from Echols' `A(37,8,8) >= 2832`),
not the 494312 in Cohn's table. Brouwer's page still lists 566652 and 755988 for dims 38
and 39, which is why the results of sections 7 and 10 remain unclaimed.

## 19. The dimension-29 slack of section 3 is (almost certainly) not realisable

Section 3 showed a purely counting gain of up to +330 for dimension 29, from schemes where
the 40 directions of `D5` carry a *load-balanced* family of triples instead of a partition.
Made concrete: a family of triples in which every direction lies in exactly `r` of them
gives, for blocks of `b` lines,

    gain = 4 * (40r/3) * b,     with  r*b <= 248  (the class bound),

so **any** improvement over the partition needs `r*b >= 242`: every one of the 40 direction
classes must be a **near-maximum** class, within 2.5% of the record 248. Concretely
`r = 3, b = 81` (40 triples, 40 classes of 243 lines) or `r = 6, b = 41` (80 triples, 40
classes of 246). The `D5` sub-family exists: an ILP finds 40 of the 80 triples with every
direction in exactly 3, and the resulting compatibility graph on blocks is 6-regular with
120 edges (`research/data/d5_triples_r3.npy`).

**The obstruction is rigidity, and it is measurable.** Each block must lie in `r` *different*
near-maximum classes. Taking a random third of the known 248-line class:

| block | lines compatible with the whole block | best class through the block, avoiding the original |
|---|---|---|
| 83 lines | 734 - 1004 | **139 - 148** (need 243) |
| 41 lines | 11850 | **143** (need 246) |

A third of a maximum class therefore determines it: the candidate set contains exactly one
large independent set -- the rest of the original class -- and the next best is barely half
the size. So the 40 near-maximum classes with prescribed shared thirds do not exist, and the
+330 is a counting bound with no configuration behind it. Dimension 29 stays at 209496.

Sharper still: sampling 16384 arenas (4096 sign changes x 4 Golay automorphisms), a random
83-line third of the maximum class is contained in **exactly one** of them. Since every
near-maximum class lies in an arena, a block of that size can belong to at most one
near-maximum class, and the `r = 3` scheme needs each block to belong to three. Dead.

## 20. The internal design of a maximum class (a new handle on section 2)

The 248 lines of the maximum class split into 124 orthogonal pairs (this was known); what is
new is what the **pair sums** look like. Every sum `u+v` has squared norm 64, i.e. is a
norm-8 (frame-type) vector, and grouping the 124 sums by their coset in `Lambda/2Lambda`:

* they occupy **14 of the 15 non-zero elements** of the 4-dimensional isotropic `R`
  (one element of `R` is unused);
* the multiplicities are **three cosets with 12 pairs and eleven with 8**
  (3*12 + 11*8 = 124);
* inside one coset the pair sums are **pairwise orthogonal** -- they are part of that
  coset's frame of 48 norm-8 vectors (24 orthogonal pairs).

So a class is exactly a choice, for each `r` in `R \ {0}`, of `m_r` of the 24 orthogonal
pairs of the frame `F_r`, subject to the class condition across cosets, and

    |class| = 2 * sum_r m_r ,    currently  m = (12,12,12,8,8,8,8,8,8,8,8,8,8,8,0).

The ceiling `m_r <= 24` would allow `2*15*24 = 720` lines, and the LP bound of section 2 is
425, so the binding constraint is entirely the cross-coset interaction. **This is the right
coordinate system in which to search for a class of more than 248 lines**: 15 small choices
of `m_r <= 24` inside frames, rather than 248 lines out of 6120. Not yet exploited.

## 21. RESULT: dimension 47 -- the table is a factor 2.44 low (tau_47 >= 23766960)

Cohn's table lists **9741412** for dimension 47 (Edel-Rains-Sloane with Brouwer's
constant-weight codes). The correct value is **23766960**.

**Construction (classical).** Let `L` be an extremal even unimodular lattice of dimension
48 (`P_48p`, `P_48q`, `P_48m`, `P_48n`); it has minimum 6 and 52416000 minimal vectors.
Fix a minimal vector `u`; the minimal vectors orthogonal to `u` lie in a 47-dimensional
space, have equal norms, and satisfy `|v-w|^2 >= 6 = |v|^2`, i.e. `cos <= 1/2`. They are a
47-dimensional kissing configuration.

**The count is forced** by three facts and nothing else:

1. an extremal even unimodular 48-dimensional lattice has exactly 52416000 minimal vectors;
2. its minimal shell is a spherical **11-design** (Venkov);
3. `v.w` in `{0,+-1,+-2,+-3}` for distinct minimal vectors, plus `+-6` for `w = +-v`.

Fact 2 gives `sum_v <u,v>^{2k} = N (2k-1)!!/(n(n+2)...(n+2k-2))` for `2k <= 11`; moments
2, 4 and 6 determine the three unknown counts, and the **8th moment then checks out
exactly**:

    A(cos = 1/6) = 12608784,  A(cos = 1/3) = 1678887,  A(cos = 1/2) = 36848,
    A(cos = 0)   = 23766960     (each sign)

`research/dim47/derive.py` does this in exact rational arithmetic and validates the same
computation on `E8 -> E7` (126), Barnes-Wall (1710) and `Leech -> Lambda_23` (93150).
The minimal vector is the best cross-section vector: `w` of norm 8 gives 20582240 and of
norm 10 gives 18409300 (the design identity holds for any fixed `w`).

**This is in the literature** -- it is equation (3) of Boyvalenkov & Cherkashin,
arXiv:2312.05121 (Dec 2023), which says in the same paragraph that these `A_0` vectors
"define a 47-dimensional kissing configuration". The table simply has not absorbed it.

**Systematic sweep of the whole family.** Extremal even unimodular lattices exist in every
dimension divisible by 8; their kissing numbers and cross-sections are

| dim | min | kissing | cross-section -> dim-1 | table at dim-1 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8  | 2 | 240      | 126        | 126 (equal) |
| 16 | 2 | 480      | 366        | 2564 |
| 24 | 4 | 196560   | 93150      | 93150 (equal) |
| 32 | 4 | 146880   | 80910      | 238350 |
| 40 | 4 | 39600    | (3-design only) | 756116 |
| **48** | **6** | **52416000** | **23766960** | **9741412** |

Only dimension 48 has minimum 6, which is why it is the only one whose cross-section wins.

**Open follow-up: dimensions 41-46.** These are cross-sections of `L` by sublattices of
rank 2 to 7. Their sizes are *not* determined by the 11-design property -- one needs the
Siegel theta series of degree >= 2 (Ozeki, Tsukuba J. Math. 40 (2016) 139-186). Note the
warning from the Leech chain: the double cross-section by two *orthogonal* minimal vectors
gives only 43164, while `Lambda_22` has 49896 -- so the right rank-2 sublattice is not
spanned by orthogonal minimal vectors. A second-moment bound gives dimension 46 at least
about 5.56 million (table: 5318060) if the 47-dimensional cross-section is a spherical
2-design, which does *not* follow from Venkov and is not automatic: `Aut(P_48q)` has order
only about 2*103776 and is not transitive on the 52416000 minimal vectors, so there is no
symmetry argument. Worse, the design property is **not** automatic: tested on explicit data,
the Leech cross-section (93150 vectors in dimension 23) is a full 6-design, but the
Barnes-Wall cross-section (1710 vectors in dimension 15) is only a **2-design** -- its 4th
moment is 30 against the design value 20.12. So the Leech case is special (Co_0 is huge),
and for `P_48`, whose automorphism group is small, neither the 4-design nor even the
2-design property can be assumed.

Two things are nevertheless certain about the 47-dimensional cross-section `C_47`:
it is **not** a 6-design (a 6-design would force `sum_v (w.v)^6` to equal
`46656 * 51600/17`, which is not an integer, while the left side is), and *if* it were a
4-design the two available moment equations would give `tau_46 >= 7997790` and
`<= 11041840`. Settling dimension 46 therefore needs the degree-2 Siegel theta series, not
design theory.

## 22. RESULTS: dimensions 45 and 46, from k-point moments of the 11-design

Section 21 used Venkov's 11-design property with polynomials in a *single* inner product,
which gives the one-point distribution and hence dimension 47. The design property is far
stronger than that: it applies to **every** polynomial of degree at most 11, in particular
to products across several fixed vectors. That yields genuine multi-point information.

Fix `k` pairwise orthogonal minimal vectors `u_1..u_k` of an extremal even unimodular
48-dimensional lattice and write `x_i = <u_i,v>/6`. For every exponent vector with
`sum a_i <= 5`,

    sum_{v in shell} x_1^{2a_1} ... x_k^{2a_k}  =  N * (2a_1-1)!!...(2a_k-1)!! /
                                                    (n(n+2)...(n+2 sum a_i - 2))

Let `n[s_1..s_k]` be the number of minimal vectors with the prescribed inner products
(`s_i` in `{0,+-1/6,+-1/3,+-1/2}`, plus the `2k` vectors `+-u_i`). Those moments, together
with the exact one-point marginals of section 21, antipodal symmetry and non-negativity,
form a linear system. Minimising `n[0,...,0]` -- the number of minimal vectors orthogonal
to all `k`, i.e. the kissing number of the `(48-k)`-dimensional cross-section -- is a
linear program:

| k | dim | Gram of the u_i | certified bound | Cohn's table | gain |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 47 | - | **23766960** | 9741412 | x 2.44 |
| 2 | 46 | one 60-degree pair | **12086040** | 5318060 | x 2.27 |
| 2 | 46 | orthogonal pair | 10659120 | 5318060 | x 2.00 |
| 3 | 45 | Gram (3,-2,0) | **4980129** | 3047160 | x 1.63 |
| 3 | 45 | 60-degree pair + orthogonal | 4636821 | 3047160 | x 1.52 |
| 3 | 45 | three orthogonal | 4344480 | 3047160 | x 1.43 |
| 4 | 44 | any | 0 | 2948552 | too underdetermined |

**The Gram matrix of the u_i matters a lot, and so does realizability.** For the Leech the
best rank-2 cross-section is NOT the orthogonal pair (43164) but the 60-degree pair
(49896 = Lambda_22) -- the LP reproduces both exactly. The same holds here: a 60-degree
pair gives 12086040 against 10659120 for an orthogonal pair.

But a sweep over all Gram matrices produces tempting values that are **vacuous**: the best
apparent dimension-45 bound, 9397794 with off-diagonals (-3,-3,-2), cannot be used, because
a triple with that Gram is not known to exist. Realizability has to be proved, and the LP
proves it: a Gram is realizable if the corresponding lower-order cell has LP minimum > 0.
Only two families pass -- pairwise orthogonal vectors, and a 60-degree pair extended by
orthogonal ones (cells with minima 23766960 and 12086040). Everything else has LP minimum
0. The `k = 2` orthogonal LP interval is `[10659120, 10697520]`, so that cross-section is
pinned to within 0.4%.

**Rigour.** The floating-point LP is used only to *find* a dual vector; the reported bound
is then certified exactly -- the dual is rounded to rationals, repaired into exact dual
feasibility by adjusting the all-ones moment row (whose coefficients are all positive), and
`b^T y` is evaluated in exact rational arithmetic. Weak duality makes any exactly
dual-feasible `y` a valid bound. `research/dim45-46/derive.py`.

**Validation.** The identical computation on the Leech lattice (also an 11-design) returns
93150 for `k = 1` and 43164 for `k = 2` -- exactly the true cross-section sizes computed
independently from explicit coordinates -- and a valid, slightly loose, 19530 <= 19962 for
`k = 3`. See `research/exp/kpoint_val.py`.

**Why this had not been done.** The one-point distribution of these lattices is classical
(Ozeki; Boyvalenkov-Cherkashin quote it), and the 47-dimensional cross-section is noted in
the literature. What is new is using the design property *multi-point*: nothing beyond
degree 11 is needed, and 11 is exactly enough for `k` up to 3 because the number of moment
equations `C(k+5,k)` overtakes the number of unknown cells only for small `k`.

**Numerical note.** The `k >= 3` linear programs are badly conditioned (moment coefficients
range over `6^-10`); HiGHS only converges on the relaxed form `|Ax-b| <= eps`. Its dual is
*automatically* feasible for the equality problem, which is what makes the certification
work: for `A_ub = [A;-A]` the returned multipliers give `y = lam_1 - lam_2` with
`A^T y <= c` exactly.


## 23. Translation constraints: the lattice structure the moments cannot see

The k-point LP of section 22 uses only that the minimal shell is an 11-design. It ignores
that the shell sits inside a *lattice*. One consequence is free and very strong:

> if `<u_i, v> = mu/2` then `|u_i - v|^2 = mu`, so `w = u_i - v` is **again a minimal
> vector**, with `<u_i,w> = mu/2` and `<u_j,w> = <u_i,u_j> - <u_j,v>`.

So `v -> u_i - v` is an involution of the set of minimal vectors whose i-th inner product is
`mu/2`, and therefore

    n[c]  =  n[c']       where   c'_i = mu/2,   c'_j = G[i][j] - c_j   (j != i),

and symmetrically `n[c] = n[c'']` via `w = u_i + v` when `c_i = -mu/2`. When the image cell
`c'` is not a legal cell (its Gram matrix is not positive semidefinite), the constraint
**forces `n[c] = 0`** outright; when `c'` is one of the `+-u_j` cells it forces `n[c] = 1`.

These are exact equalities that the pure moment method never sees, and they matter:

| case | moments only | with translations | truth |
|---|---|---|---|
| Leech, k=3, 60-degree triple | 26946 | **27720** | 27720 (`Lambda_21`) -- now exact |
| Leech, k=2, 60-degree pair | 49896 | 49896 | 49896 (already exact) |
| P_48, k=2, 60-degree pair | 12086040 | **12309600** | in [12086040, 12342720] |
| P_48, k=3, Gram (3,-2,0) | 4980129 | **5359320** | - |
| P_48, k=4 | 0 | **> 0** | - |

So the certified bounds improve to

    tau(46) >= 12309600      (table 5318060, x 2.31)
    tau(45) >=  5359320      (table 3047160, x 1.76)

and dimension 44, which the moment-only LP could not touch at all, becomes reachable.

Note the sign convention: for `c_i = -mu/2` the image is `c''_j = G[i][j] + c_j`, **not**
`-G[i][j] + c_j`. Getting this wrong makes the LP infeasible, which is how the bug was
caught.

## 24. How tight are these bounds?

Running the same LP with the opposite objective gives the interval that the true
cross-section size must lie in:

| configuration | dim | LP minimum (certified) | LP maximum |
|---|---|---|---|
| 60-degree pair | 46 | **12309600** | **12309600** -- exact |
| orthogonal pair | 46 | 10659120 | 10697520 |
| Gram (3,-2,0) | 45 | 5359320 | 6694001 |

So the *size of the configuration* in dimension 46 is determined: the 46-dimensional
cross-section of an extremal 48-dimensional lattice by a 60-degree pair of minimal vectors
has exactly 12309600 minimal vectors. **This does not make tau(46) exact** -- it makes the
lower bound tau(46) >= 12309600 non-improvable *within this construction*. The kissing
numbers themselves are known exactly only in dimensions 1, 2, 3, 4, 8 and 24; the upper
bound for dimension 46 in Cohn's table is 441900184, so the true value is wide open. Cell by cell, 31 of the 47 cells of that two-point distribution
are pinned to single values; the 16 undetermined ones all have both coordinates in
{+-1,+-2}. For the orthogonal pair nothing is determined -- the translation constraints of
section 23 only bite when some coordinate equals +-mu/2, which an orthogonal pair rarely
achieves.

A further strengthening not yet exploited: feed the determined two-point values back into
the three-point LP as exact marginal constraints. They are not automatically implied,
because the "singular cells have count <= 1" bounds of a two-point system do not follow
from the corresponding three-point bounds.

## 25. Span constraints (a generalisation of section 23, no further gain here)

The translation of section 23 works for any minimal vector w, not only the u_i. Every
minimal w in span(u_1..u_k) -- found by enumerating small integer combinations -- gives

  * an exact cell: the profile (<u_1,w>,...,<u_k,w>) is singular, so its count is at most 1,
    and w is in the lattice, so it is exactly 1;
  * a translation: if sum_i a_i c_i = mu/2 then n[c] = n[c'] with c'_l = <u_l,w> - c_l.

For the Gram matrices used here (which have 6 to 20 such w) this reproduces the section-23
values exactly and adds nothing further, but it is the right general form and is what
`research/exp/kgram3.py` implements.

## 26. Groundwork for multi-shell constraints (the next lever)

Every shell of an extremal even unimodular lattice is an 11-design, not just the minimal
one, and the shells are linked by the lattice structure. The extremal 48-dimensional theta
series begins

    1 + 52416000 q^3 + 39007332000 q^4 + ...

so the second shell (norm 8) has N_8 = 39007332000 vectors. Its one-point distribution
relative to a *minimal* vector u follows from its own 11-design property (four unknowns,
four moments, and the tenth moment as a free check, which passes):

    |u.w| = 1 : 19075585536      |u.w| = 3 : 286530048
    |u.w| = 2 :  4324812912      |u.w| = 4 :      3357774
    u.w   = 0 : 15317045730

Consistency check that ties the two shells together: `u.w = 4` means `|u-w|^2 = 6`, so those
w are exactly `u - v` for minimal v with `u.v = 2`; and indeed 3357774 is precisely the
minimal shell's `A(cos 1/3)` count. The bijection is exact.

That bijection generalises the translations of section 23: for a k-point cell of the minimal
shell with `c_i = 2`, the map `v -> u_i - v` lands in the norm-8 shell at the cell with
`c'_i = 4`, `c'_j = G_ij - c_j`. Joining the two shells' k-point systems through these
equations, and adding the norm-8 shell's own moments and its own translations (`c'_i = 3`
maps shell 8 to itself), is the natural next strengthening. It is the obvious route to
narrowing dimension 45, whose interval is still [5359320, 6694001].

Two strengthenings that were tried and gave **nothing**:

* feeding the 31 exactly-determined cells of the 60-degree-pair two-point distribution into
  the three-point LP as marginal constraints -- already implied;
* the span constraints of section 25 -- also already implied for these Gram matrices.

## 27. INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATION of the dimension-46 number, from Ozeki's Siegel theta series

M. Ozeki, *A numerical study of Siegel theta series of various degrees for the
48-dimensional even unimodular extremal lattices*, Tsukuba J. Math. 40 (2016) 139-186,
tabulates the degree-3 Fourier coefficients. Table 3 row 9 gives

    a( (3,3,3,3,0,0) ) = 23775066324172800000 ,   a( (3,3,3) ) = 1931424768000

and the quotient is exactly **12309600** -- the number the linear program of section 22
produced. Two completely independent routes (modular forms vs. an 11-design LP with an
exact rational dual certificate) agree to the digit. Ozeki's degree-2 table also reproduces
the one-point distribution of section 21 exactly: a((3,3,c)) = 52416000 x A_c for
c = 3,2,1,0.

Three further facts from that table, all of which corroborate the LP:

* **pair at inner product 2** -> 11306800 minimal vectors orthogonal to both;
  **pair at inner product 1** -> 10811360. Both are smaller than 12309600, confirming
  independently that the 60-degree pair is the best rank-2 cross-section (section 22 found
  this by sweeping the Gram matrix).
* **the orthogonal pair is genuinely non-constant.** Table 3 row 20 divided by a((3,3,0))
  gives 458386320/43 = 10660146.98..., *not an integer*. Since that is an exact average over
  all ordered orthogonal pairs, the number of minimal vectors orthogonal to a given
  orthogonal pair varies from pair to pair. So the LP interval [10659120, 10697520] for that
  configuration **cannot** be collapsed to a single value -- the bracket is a feature of the
  geometry, not a weakness of the method.
* the dimension-45 index is **not** in the literature: Ozeki's degree-4 Table 5 stops at
  discriminant 720, and the Gram (3,-3,0) used here has discriminant 828. Moreover his
  Theorem 6.2 shows every degree-4 coefficient has the form panth(T) + t J^3(T) with a
  single global unknown t (bounded by Theorem 6.3 to within a factor 278/184), so degree-4
  counts are in general lattice-dependent. Dimension 45 therefore genuinely needs the LP.

## 28. Landscape: what the current records actually are, and where they came from

* **dims 25-31** were all improved in Nov 2025 by Ma et al., arXiv:2511.13391
  ("PackingStar", game-theoretic reinforcement learning), superseding Kallal-Kan-Wang 2017.
  Their form is `196560 + m*496 + tau_{n-24}` with m = 1, 4, 7, 16, 26, 48, 84. Our dim-27
  value 200540 = 196560 + **8**\*496 + 12 is exactly one 496-block more than theirs, and our
  dim-25 value 197058 is theirs plus the two poles of section 13.
* **dims 17-21**: Cohn-Li, arXiv:2411.04916, get 5730, 7654, 11692, 19448, 29768 by flipping
  the *parity of the sign count* in the Steiner-system construction (an "odd" configuration
  instead of the "even" one that gives the Leech cross-sections), which opens room for extra
  vectors indexed by a subcode of the punctured Golay code with minimum distance >= n/4.
  Ho, arXiv:2603.10425, then improved dim 19 to **11948** with a nonlinear such subcode
  found via a 5-coclique in the Clebsch graph.
* **that mechanism is now provably exhausted**: the maximum number of addable vectors is
  exactly 2048 in dimensions 20 and 21 (in dim 21 the forbidden-difference set is the 21
  lines of PG(2,4), all of odd weight, so the Cayley graph is bipartite and regular, hence
  has a perfect matching and independence number exactly half; in dim 20 the five forbidden
  words partition the coordinates and the Cayley graph is the 5-cube). Dimensions 18, 22, 23,
  24 fall short of their records even using the entire ambient code. **Only dimension 19 is
  open, with a ceiling of 10668 + 4*384 = 12204, i.e. at most +256 over Ho.**
* Cohn's table is stale for **dims 32, 33, 34, 37**: Echols (arXiv:2608.13906) gives
  346432, 362048, 381124, 496232 from new A(n,8,8) codes, and Brouwer's page carries them.
* dim 43 comes from the Sun-Wang antipode construction (arXiv:2607.20359); dims 41, 42, 44
  from Edel-Rains-Sloane with Brouwer's codes.
* small dimensions 2025-26: k(11) 592 -> 593 (AlphaEvolve) -> **604** (arXiv:2606.10402);
  k(12) 840 -> **841** (arXiv:2606.18984). Dims 9, 13, 15, 16 unchanged.

## 29. Lattice-range constraints, and the complete degree-3 table

A constraint the positive-semidefiniteness test misses. For **every** lattice vector
`w = sum a_i u_i` in the span and every minimal `v`,

    |v - w|^2 = mu + |w|^2 - 2<v,w>   is a lattice norm, so   |<v,w>| <= |w|^2 / 2

(with the single exception `v = +-w`, possible only when `|w|^2 = mu`). A cell violating this
for some `w` is **empty**. PSD alone does not see this: for a 60-degree pair, `w = u1 - u2`
is itself minimal, so `|c_1 - c_2| <= 3`, which kills the cells with `|c_1 - c_2|` equal to
4 or 5. Implemented in `research/exp/kgram5.py`; it removes exactly 10 of the 47 cells,
**precisely Ozeki's Lemma-4.2 zero set** -- an independent derivation of it.

Ozeki's complete degree-3 table for a 60-degree pair (`l_{a,b} = a(T)/a((3,3,3))`,
`a((3,3,3)) = 1931424768000`):

```
  a\b |    -3      -2      -1        0       1       2       3
 -----+---------------------------------------------------------
   -3 |  1656   16767   16767     1656       0       0   [deg]
   -2 | 16767  361584  922185   361584   16767       0       0
   -1 | 16767  922185 5365440  5365440  922185   16767       0
    0 |  1656  361584 5365440 12309600 5365440  361584    1656
    1 |     0   16767  922185  5365440 5365440  922185   16767
    2 |     0       0   16767   361584  922185  361584   16767
    3 | [deg]      0       0     1656   16767   16767    1656
```

47 cells summing to 52415994, plus the six degenerate vectors `+-u1, +-u2, +-(u1-u2)`, total
exactly 52416000. My LP determines 31 of these cells and **agrees with Ozeki on every one**;
the lattice-range constraint above then forces the 10 zeros as well.

**Imposing the whole table on the three-point LP changes nothing**: dimension 45 stays at
6462480 for Gram (3,-3,0), 5359320 for (3,-2,0), 5194969 for A_3. So the LP is already
extracting everything the degree-3 data contains, and the residual gap to the LP maximum
7137936 is genuinely degree-4 information -- which, by Ozeki's Theorem 6.2, carries a global
unknown and is in general lattice-dependent. That the LP remains feasible with Ozeki's exact
values imposed is itself a further consistency check on the whole construction.

## 30. Delsarte bounds for arccos(1/3)-codes, and why dimension 12 is closed to layering

The head codes of the layered small-dimension constructions are arccos(1/3)-codes: unit
vectors with pairwise cos <= 1/3 (angle >= 70.5288 degrees). Delsarte LP bounds, and the best
constructions I know (all obtained as *caps* of record 60-degree codes -- see section 26 --
and verified to have maximum cosine exactly 1/3):

| n | best construction | Delsarte LP (mine) | best published upper bound |
|---|---|---|---|
| 7 | 56 | 56 | **56 (exact,** tight 7-design) |
| 8 | 64 | 78 | 78 |
| 9 | 96 (SPLAG) / 82 (cap) | 108 | **107** |
| 10 | **108** (cap of the dim-11 record) | 146 | **145** |
| 11 | 82 | 194 | 194 |
| 12 | 118 | 257 | 257 |
| 13 | - | 338 | **337** |
| 23 | 4600 (Leech cap) | - | 4600 (exact) |

The sharpened upper bounds are Boyvalenkov & Kazakov, *New upper bounds for some spherical
codes*, Serdica Math. J. **21** (1995) 231-238, Example 6 (free PDF at
math.bas.bg/serdica/1995/1995-231-238.pdf). Their method is not a new LP polynomial: they
show a code *attaining* the degree-5 Levenshtein bound cannot exist, because the distance
distribution forced by the Vandermonde system fails integrality -- hence bounds drop by
exactly one. They rule out (9,108,1/3), (10,146,1/3), (13,338,1/3), (15,576,1/3),
(16,752,1/3), (17,986,1/3). Nothing at all for n = 11 or 12.
Conway-Sloane, SPLAG Table 9.2 (p. 250), tabulates `A(n, arccos 1/3)` for n <= 10 as the
"second kissing number" -- how many spheres can touch two touching spheres in `R^{n+1}`; its
n = 9 entry is the two-sided 96-107, and n = 10 carries only the upper bound. My cap
construction reproduces its exact entries `A(7,1/3) = 56` and `A(23,1/3) = 4600`
independently, which validates the cap principle of section 26.

**Dimension 12 cannot be reached by layering.** The layered construction from the dimension-11
record gives `604 - d + 2h + 2`, so beating 841 needs `2h - d >= 238`; with heads drawn from
the equator (d = h, the cheapest possible) that means `h >= 238` in R^11, far above the LP
bound 194. Even a *free* head set (d = 0, which cannot happen since the covering radius of
the dim-11 code exceeds the threshold) would need h >= 119, still above the best known 82.
So dimension 12's 841 is not reachable this way, and the numerical record stands alone.

**Dimension 11 remains the live small-dimension lever**, exactly as recorded in section 11:
`510 - d + 2h + 2` with the known h = 108 and d = 124 gives the record 604, and the LP
allows h up to 146. An arccos(1/3)-code in R^10 with h = 146 and d ~ 1.15h = 168 would give
636. Any h > 108 with proportionate d improves on 604.

## 31. Dimension 19: the structure behind Ho's 320, and why it resists

`k(19) = 10668 + 4*alpha(Cay(K,S))`, where `D` is the 5-punctured extended Golay code
(length 19, 4096 words, minimum weight 3), `S` is the set of nonzero words of weight below
19/4 -- i.e. weight 3 or 4 -- and `K = span(S)` has dimension 10 and index 4 in `D`.
Ho (arXiv:2603.10425) achieves `alpha = 320`, giving 11948; the Delsarte LP ceiling is 384,
giving 12204. So a new record needs `alpha >= 321`, and `alpha = 384` would need a subgroup
`M` of dimension 6 with quotient independence number 6.

**What S actually is** (derived here, and it explains everything). Puncture five coordinates.
By the Steiner system `S(5,8,24)`:

* there is a **unique** octad through all five punctured points; after puncturing it has
  weight 3 -- that is the single weight-3 word;
* through any four of the five there are exactly five octads, one of which is that special
  one; the other four have weight 4 after puncturing. With five choices of the 4-subset
  `Q`, that is exactly `5 x 4 = 20` weight-4 words. Total `|S| = 21`. Confirmed
  computationally.

**And the key fact:** for each 4-subset `Q`, the five octads through `Q` partition the other
twenty points, so after puncturing their restrictions **partition the 19 coordinates**,
`3 + 4 + 4 + 4 + 4 = 19`. That is precisely the source of the Clebsch connection set
`{e1, e2, e3, e4, e1+e2+e3+e4}` -- five elements summing to zero -- which Ho found; the
