  first codimension at which the number of parts reaches the number of disjoint classes the
  Leech shell can be split into -- which is precisely why the Leech route peaks there.
* **98 280 lines is the whole shell.** No line is unused, so the 4*L term cannot grow.

The only slack is the axis layer: 1908 against a ceiling of tau(14) = 1932, worth at most
+24. It is genuinely constrained rather than merely unoptimised -- at cap level t = 2/3 an
axis point must sit at least 30 degrees from EVERY one of the 1932 cap directions
(sqrt(1-t)*<z,w> <= 1/2 means <z,w> <= sqrt(3)/2), and the cap directions already fill R^14.

**Why the Leech route is confined to dimension 38.** The gain is at most
4 * 98 280 = 393 120, so dimension 24+k is at most 589 680 + tau(k) by this route:

| k | 8 | 12 | 13 | 14 | 15 | 16 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| dim | 32 | 36 | 37 | **38** | 39 | 40 |
| parts M(k) available | 80 | 252 | 306 | **644** | 780 | 1440 |
| ceiling | 589 920 | 590 521 | 590 834 | **591 612** | 592 244 | 594 000 |
| record | 345 408 | 484 568 | 494 312 | **566 652** | 755 988 | 1 064 368 |

Below k = 14 the number of parts M(k) is the binding constraint (only 80 classes may be used
at k = 8, so the gain caps near 79 000); above k = 15 the shell itself is exhausted at 98 280
lines while the records keep growing. Dimension 38 is the unique crossing point. Dimension 39's
756 116 comes from the Edel-Rains-Sloane route with refreshed constant-weight codes, not from
this one.


## 58. A REAL BUG: the k-point LP was missing the mixed moments (2026-08-21)

`kgram5.py` and its descendants imposed only those moments in which **every** exponent is
even -- 56 equations for k = 3, from `sum a_i <= 5` applied to
`x_1^{2a_1} ... x_k^{2a_k}`. But Venkov's theorem says the minimal shell is a spherical
11-design, so **every** polynomial of degree <= 11 integrates exactly, including the mixed
ones. The correct family is every exponent vector (p_1,...,p_k) with

    sum p_i <= 11  and  sum p_i EVEN

(odd total degree vanishes by antipodal symmetry), with coefficient `prod (c_i/mu)^{p_i}` and
sphere moment `wick(p, Gn)/prod_{j<sum p/2}(n+2j)`. For k = 3 that is **161 equations, not
56**. These are exactly what Ozeki obtains by expanding `sum_c l_c (au+bv+ct)^{2k}`.

**The bug was visible in my own output and I had not noticed.** The Grams `(3,-3,0)` and
`3*A_3` are equivalent -- `U^T G U = 3*A_3` for `U = [[-1,-1,0],[0,0,-1],[-1,0,0]]`, det -1 --
so they must give the same answer. Mine gave **6462480 and 5194969**. With the mixed moments
both give **6687320**. Any future version of this machinery should be checked on a pair of
equivalent Grams; it is a cheap and decisive test.

**Effects, all verified:**

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| Leech k=2 orthogonal / 60-degree | 43164 / 49896 exact | unchanged, still exact |
| Leech k=3 60-degree | 27720 exact | unchanged |
| dimension 46 (P_48, k=2) | 12309600 | unchanged -- it was already exact |
| dimension 47 (P_48, k=1) | 23766960 | unchanged -- k = 1 has no mixed moments |
| dimension 71 (Gamma_72, k=1) | 2603658750 | unchanged, same reason |
| **dimension 45** (P_48, k=3) | 6462480 | **6687320** (+224 840) |
| **dimension 70** (Gamma_72, k=2) | 1170823530 | **1249778250** (+78 954 720) |

So the one-point results were never affected, and the two- and three-point ones were.

**It also re-derives Ozeki's Theorem 6.3 independently.** With mixed moments the three-point
system for a 60-degree triple in an extremal 48-dimensional lattice has affine solution space
of dimension exactly **1**, with

    n[0,0,0] + 164 * n[3,3,3] = 6732912   (per triple, exact)

and non-negativity forces `184 <= n[3,3,3] <= 278`, binding at the cells (3,3,1) and (3,3,0).
That is Ozeki's bound on tau = a(T4,3)/a(T3,1), obtained by linear programming with an exact
rational dual certificate rather than by modular forms.

## 59. Dimension 45 pinned to 0.23%, and dimension 44 falls

**The index identification in section 27 was wrong.** Ozeki's discriminant convention is
`D = det(2T)/2` in degree 3 and `D = det(2T)` in degree 4 (his section 5.1.1). My Gram
`[[6,3,-3],[3,6,0],[-3,0,6]]` has determinant 108, so **D = 54, not 69**: it is row 1 of his
Table 3, form (3,3,3,3,3,0), `a(T3,1) = 3198439415808000`, and it is his own "First Case"
index. Cross-check: `3198439415808000 / 1931424768000 = 1656`, the (3,3) cell of my own
degree-3 table in section 29.

Consequently the quaternary discriminant is **648, not 828** -- and 648 is inside his Table 5,
row T4,33, with `J^3 = -164` and pan-theta `21534811123966672896000`. (No entry of Table 5 is
zero; the earlier reading that some vanish came from a mis-aligned PDF extraction in which the
minus sign is an unmapped glyph that `pdftotext` silently drops.) Since
`21534811123966672896000 / 3198439415808000 = 6732912` exactly, this is **Proposition
5.3(xii)**:

    a(T4,33) = 6732912 * a(T3,1) - 164 * a(T4,3),

so with `tau = a(T4,3)/a(T3,1)` in [184, 278],

    N = 6732912 - 164*tau  in  [6 687 320, 6 702 736],  N = 56 (mod 164), 95 possible values.

**tau(45) >= 6 687 320**, up from 6 462 480, and the residual uncertainty falls from 675 456
to **15 416 (0.23%)**.

**Dimension 44 falls out of the same analysis.** The quaternary index T4,2 (D = 400) is
realizable for every admissible tau, and gives a 44-dimensional cross-section in
[3 732 828, 3 735 648], so **tau(44) >= 3 732 828** against Cohn's 2 948 552 -- a factor 1.27.
A pleasant equivalence also drops out: *3*D_4 embeds in an extremal 48-dimensional lattice iff
tau <= 277*, because that configuration sits at cell (3,3,0) whose count is `278 - tau`. If
that is ever settled, dimension 44 jumps to 4 147 968 and dimension 45 to 6 687 484.

**What will not pin tau** (all tried, all negative): the 4-point LP on 3*A_4 (781 cells, 2279
rows) still gives exactly [184, 278]; and the two-shell norm-6 plus norm-8 LP -- section 26's
"next lever" -- gives exactly [184, 278] as well, so that lever is now exhausted for this
quantity. All 20 rows of Ozeki's Table 3 have `a(T) > 0`, so every triple-class is realized
and no separate realizability argument is needed; D = 54 wins outright, the runner-up D = 60
reaching only 6 344 672.

**The only route left** is an explicit enumeration: `tau = #{minimal y : <y,u_i> = 3}` for one
60-degree triple in P_48p or P_48q -- a closest-vector computation in the rank-45 orthogonal
complement (determinant 108, minimum 6) at radius^2 15/4. That needs fpylll, PARI or Magma,
none of which is installed here.

**Literature, checked:** there is **no published value of a(T4,3)** for any 48-dimensional
extremal lattice, and it is **not known** whether Theta_4 differs among P48p/q/m/n -- Salvati
Manni's theorem says the difference is a multiple of J^3 "up to a multiplicative constant,
possibly 0", so claims that he showed them to differ are wrong. The dimension-32 analogue
*was* settled (Oura-Ozeki, Abh. Hamburg 86 (2016) 19-53: five distinct values), but by
counting quadruples of weight-8 binary codewords, which does not transfer to dimension 48.


## 60. Dimensions 32-44 are exactly at the Edel-Rains-Sloane value (2026-08-21)

Maximised over ALL admissible chains with EVERY currently published lower bound on A(n,d,w)
and A(m,d), the Edel-Rains-Sloane construction reproduces the best known kissing number in
every dimension 32-44 and improves none. Package `research/dim32-44/`.

| n | best chain | N(n) | Cohn's table | best known | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 32 | (32,8,2) | 346432 | 345408 | 346432 | at the value |
| 35, 36, 40 | (32/40,8,2) | = table | = | = | at the value |
| 37 | (32,8,2) | **496232** | 494312 | 496232 | at the value |
| 38 | (38,8,2) | 570236 | 566652 | **591588** (this project) | below |
| 39 | (39,8,2) | 756116 | 755988 | 756116 (this project) | at the value |
| 41, 42 | **(40,8,2)** | = table | = | = | at the value |
| 43 | (43,8,2) | 1745692 | 2060399 | 2060399 | below |
| 44 | (44,8,2) | 2948552 | 2948552 | **3732828** (this project) | below |

Two things worth keeping.

**Cohn's table is stale in six of these dimensions** -- 32, 33, 34, 37 by Echols' 2026
constant-weight improvements, 38 and 39 by this project. In particular **dimension 37's live
record is 496 232, not Cohn's 494 312**, confirming the standing memory note. Brouwer's
Andw.html (fetched 2026-08-21, citing Echols' emails through 2026-08-08) is the current
authority for A(n,8,8): 1667, 1788, 1936, 2157, 2742, 2832, 3025, 3324, 3683, 4510, 5136,
5418, 6622 for n = 32..44.

**The chain choice is non-obvious.** Dimensions 41 and 42 optimise at n_0 = 40, not n_0 = n,
because A(41,11) = 2^18 and A(42,11) = 2^19 both sit BELOW A(40,10) = 589824. Anyone
evaluating this construction should enumerate the chains rather than assume n_0 = n.

**Where the leverage is.** Every dimension except 38 and 43 sits exactly at the ERS value, so
ANY code improvement is immediately a record: +1 in A(n,8,8) gives +128 in tau_n; +1 in
A(32,8) gives +1 in tau_32 ... tau_37 simultaneously; +1 in A(40,10) gives +1 in tau_40,
tau_41, tau_42 simultaneously. The middle levels are hopeless -- weight 9 is killed outright
by the Johnson bound, and for weights 10 and 11 nothing at all is published above n = 32,
where beating the weight-8 level at n = 44 would need A(44,10,10) >= 11773, about 24 times the
largest published value. codetables.de gives d(42,20) = d(43,21) = d(44,22) = 10 exactly, so
no linear code can lift the top-level sign codes either.


## 61. 3*D_4 found in P_48p: tau <= 277, and dimension 44 jumps (2026-08-21)

Section 59 left dimension 44 at 3 732 828 with a conditional improvement to 4 147 968 that
held **iff** 3*D_4 embeds in an extremal 48-dimensional lattice, equivalently iff
a(T4,1) > 0, equivalently iff tau <= 277 -- because that configuration sits at cell (3,3,0)
of the 60-degree triple, whose count is exactly 278 - tau.

Rather than compute tau (which needs a rank-45 closest-vector enumeration and no
fpylll/PARI/Magma is installed), it is enough to EXHIBIT one. Searching the 7 501 227-line
pool of P_48p minimal vectors built for the dimension 49-63 work
(`research/dim45-siegel/find3D4.py`) found one **on the first attempt**, at pool indices
(6380725, 747791, 6047885, 510377). Re-verified in exact integer arithmetic: the four vectors
are integral, all of norm exactly 6, and their Gram is

    [[ 6,-3, 0, 0],
     [-3, 6,-3,-3],
     [ 0,-3, 6, 0],
     [ 0,-3, 0, 6]]  =  3 * (the D_4 Cartan matrix),  determinant 324.

Hence:

* **tau <= 277**, so tau lies in [184, 277] and the value tau = 278 is excluded;
* **tau(44) >= 4 147 968** unconditionally -- against Cohn's 2 948 552, a factor **1.41**,
  and up from the 3 732 828 of section 59;
* **tau(45) >= 6 687 484** (= 6732912 - 164*277), a further +164.

The lesson generalises: when a Siegel coefficient's positivity is what gates a bound, finding
one explicit configuration in a pool of minimal vectors is enormously cheaper than evaluating
the coefficient. The pool built for one purpose (classes for the cap construction) answered a
completely different question.

**Still open on tau.** The remaining bracket is [184, 277], 94 values, and pinning it exactly
would fix dimension 45 within 15 252 and dimension 44 within 17 664. The route is the
enumeration `tau = #{minimal y : <y,u_i> = 3 for a 60-degree triple}` -- a coset of a rank-45
lattice of determinant 108, at squared radius 15/4 (the projection of y onto the triple's span
is forced, with squared norm 9/4). The same pool trick will not settle it, because the pool is
a subset of the shell and a count needs the whole cell.


## 62. The dimension-19 graph has girth 5, and is a 64-fold cover of the Clebsch graph

Reading Ho's paper (arXiv:2603.10425) rather than inferring it gives the construction exactly,
and it matches the reduction of section 52 to the letter. His chain is

    M <= K <= D,   |M| = 64,  |K/M| = 16,  |D/K| = 4

with D the ambient 5-punctured Golay code (4096), K the span of its low-weight words
(1024 -- my component), and M a 6-dimensional subcode. **The 21 forbidden words lie in exactly
five nonzero M-cosets**, and those five cosets make K/M = F_2^4 into the **Clebsch graph**,
SRG(16,5,0,2), whose independence number is exactly 5. A 5-coclique lifts to 5*64 = 320 in K
and 4*320 = 1280 in D.

**New structural facts computed here.** The connection set S is 1 word of weight 3 (the
punctured octad through the five deleted points) and 20 of weight 4 (5 groups of 4, one group
per 4-subset of the deleted 5-set). And:

* **no two elements of S sum to an element of S** -- the graph is TRIANGLE-FREE;
* **no four sum to zero** -- there are no 4-cycles either, so the **girth is 5**, with 240
  pentagons through each vertex.

So K is a 21-regular girth-5 graph on 1024 vertices -- comfortably above the Moore bound
1 + 21 + 21*20 = 442 -- and a 64-fold cover of the Clebsch graph whose independence ratio
320/1024 = 5/16 matches its quotient's exactly.

**Consequences for bounding alpha.**

* Girth 5 kills the odd-cycle cuts. The graph is vertex-transitive, so the LP optimum is
  attained at the symmetric point x = c, where the pentagon constraints give 5c <= 2 and the
  edges give 2c <= 1; the binding one is c = 2/5, i.e. **409.6 -- worse than theta = 384**.
  Adding odd-cycle inequalities to the stable-set relaxation cannot help here.
* Triangle-freeness makes the clique cover a matching, so that bound is only 512.
* S is **not** a union of M-cosets (21 words in 5 cosets of size 64), so K is not a complete
  blow-up of the Clebsch graph. If it were, alpha = 64*5 = 320 would follow immediately. The
  bipartite graph between two adjacent fibres is only 4- or 5-regular on 64+64, which leaves
  real freedom -- which is precisely why 321 is not obviously impossible.

Current state: 320 <= alpha <= 348, with theta = 384, CP-SAT proving 348, greedy finding 320
on the first restart, and 400 restarts of (1,2)-swap plus a k-fixed annealing search failing
to reach 321.


## 63. Dimension 11: the 604 decoded, and it is the exact ceiling of its own ansatz (2026-08-21)

Sections 11, 12, 30, 32 and 35 modelled dimension 11 as `510 - d + 2h + 2`. **That model is
wrong about the record.** The 604 is a completely explicit algebraic object; it is rebuilt
here in exact `Q(sqrt2)` arithmetic and shown to be the exact maximum of its family.

### Provenance (settled)

| dim | LB | who | ref |
|---|---|---|---|
| 10 | 510 | **Ganzhinov**, *Highly symmetric lines*, Linear Algebra Appl. **722** (2025) 12-37 | arXiv:2207.08266, section 5.4 |
| 11 | **604** | **Bianchi, Kwon, Pappu, Zou**, *Harnessing the collective intelligence of AI agents in the wild for new discoveries* | arXiv:**2606.10402** (9 Jun 2026) |
| 12 | 841 | Takhanov, Assylbekov, Yun | arXiv:2606.18984 |
| 13 | 1154 | Zinoviev-Ericson 1999 | PPI 35 (1999) 287-294 |

Upper bounds: 553 (Machado-de Oliveira Filho) for dim 10; 868 / 1355 / 2064 (de Laat-Leijenhorst
2024) for 11 / 12 / 13. Cohn's table last updated **30 July 2026**.

The dimension-11 record is **not in the mathematics literature at all** -- it is a cs.CL/cs.AI
paper about Together AI's *EinsteinArena* agent platform, which is why math.MG sweeps miss it.
Its progression is 582 (Best 1980 / Zinoviev-Ericson) -> 592 (Ganzhinov) -> 593 (AlphaEvolve,
arXiv:2506.13131) -> 594 (agents) -> **604** (agents, by extending a 496-vector backbone
algebraically). Both Takhanov papers have **zero citations**; `github.com/k-nic/841_in_12D` has
not been touched since 2026-06-22 and its `beyond841/` holds only the two sub-threshold codes
(842 at 0.500900, 844 at 0.504404). Cohn's *Table of spherical codes* gives the numerical
landscape one point up: **dim 10 N=511 -> cos 0.526701**, **dim 11 N=605 -> cos 0.507413**,
**dim 12 N=842 -> cos 0.500900**. Dimension 11 is the *hardest* of the three numerically.

### The 604, exactly (rebuilt and verified: `research/dim11-12/build604.py`)

Norm^2 = 4 throughout, so the kissing condition is `<x,y> <= 2`. Coordinates split
**base {0..7} + layer {8,9,10}**.

* matching `P_0=(0,6), P_1=(1,4), P_2=(2,5), P_3=(3,7)` of the base;
* rational orthogonal frame of the layer, `u_1=(2,1,2)/3, u_2=(2,-2,-1)/3, u_3=(1,2,-2)/3`
  (entries `+-1/3, +-2/3`);
* **axis** `+-2e_i`, i in the base: 16;
* **supports**: all 16 sign patterns of `+-1` on each 4-set of a family `S` of size 30 --
  the 6 pair-unions `P_m u P_n`, plus 24 sets `T u {k}` with `k` a layer coordinate and `T`
  a *transversal* triple (at most one point from each `P_m`), 8 per layer coordinate,
  a maximum partial triple system `D(8,3,2) = 8`, all 24 triples distinct: 480;
* **E_1** `+-e_a +- e_b +- sqrt2 u_j`, `(a,b)` in the matching: 96;
* **E_2** `sqrt2(+-u_i +- u_j)`, the cuboctahedron in the u-frame: 12.

`16 + 480 + 96 + 12 = 604`. Exact check over all 182106 pairs in `Q(sqrt2)`: 604 distinct
vectors, every norm exactly 4, **maximum inner product exactly 2**, zero violations.
(Cohn's `dimensions1-24.txt` dim-11 block is this configuration.)

### Every component is forced, and 604 is the exact maximum

* **E_1 <= 96.** For a fixed pair the layer directions must be pairwise at `<w,w'> <= 0`, so at
  most `2*ell = 6` of them in `R^3` (a signed orthonormal frame); the pairs must be pairwise
  disjoint (sharing one coordinate gives inner product 3), so at most 4; `4*4*6 = 96`.
* **E_2 <= 12 = tau(3)**, and `|<w,u_j>| <= 1/sqrt2` forces the cuboctahedron in the u-frame.
* The **three layer axis vectors are excluded by E_2** (`|w_k| = 0.9428 > 1/2`), which is why
  the axis layer is 16 and not 22.
* **Supports.** The admissible 4-sets are exactly (a) any 4-subset of the base, (b) `T u {k}`
  with `T` transversal. Two layer coordinates in one support is impossible because
  `sqrt2 *` (min pair sum of the frame) `= sqrt2 > 1`. Exact CP-SAT over all 166 candidates:
  **max |S| = 30, proved OPTIMAL** (`research/dim11-12/supports.py`). So `124 + 16*30 = 604`
  is the exact ceiling.
* **Trade curve** over `k` = number of pairs carrying a full frame, with *everything else*
  (supports, layer code, axis) solved exactly (`research/dim11-12/trade.py`):

  | k | E_1 | max \|S\| (exact) | best total | ceiling for that k |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 0 | 0 | 35 = A(11,4,4) | **582** (the 1999 Zinoviev-Ericson record) | <= 588 |
  | 1 | 24 | **33** (OPTIMAL) | 574 | <= 580 |
  | 2 | 48 | 32 (OPTIMAL) | 582 | = 582 |
  | 3 | 72 | 31 (OPTIMAL) | 590 | = 590 |
  | 4 | 96 | 30 (OPTIMAL) | **604** | **= 604** |

  The `max |S|` column is proved by CP-SAT on the exact clique formulation (`|S n S'| <= 2`
  for all pairs is *equivalent* to "every 3-set of coordinates lies in at most one block", 165
  AtMostOne constraints -- far stronger than pairwise clauses). The per-`k` ceiling adds the
  largest possible non-support part, which is `max(2*11, 2*8 + 12) = 28` (the three layer axis
  vectors and the twelve `E_2` vectors are mutually exclusive). **Every rung is below 604, and
  k = 4 attains it.** So the `sqrt2` frame buys exactly **+22** over the integral optimum, and
  604 is the exact maximum of the whole family.
* **The frame's maximum entry 2/3 is the whole trick.** A support `T u {k}` needs
  `|T n P_m| + sqrt2 |(u_j)_k| <= 2`; since `max_j |(u_j)_k| >= 1/sqrt3` for *any* orthonormal
  frame of `R^3`, `|T n P_m| <= 1` is unavoidable -- but `sqrt2 * 2/3 = 0.943 < 1` is what
  makes `|T n P_m| = 1` legal. A frame with a zero entry (the standard basis) forces
  `|T n P_m| = 0` for the other two layer coordinates and is far worse (about 348).

### What is NOT closed (be honest about it)

Two gaps remain in the "604 is the exact ceiling" claim.

* The trade curve fixes **full** 3-direction frames on `k` pairs. Partial frames and the
  standard basis were argued away by hand (dropping one direction of the 604 frame frees no
  support, because all nine of its entries are non-zero; the standard frame's zeros force
  `|T n P| = 0` at the other two layer coordinates and collapses the total to about 348), but
  the fully general block ILP over both frames (`master.py 3 T 11 605`, 539 blocks / 20652
  conflicting pairs, and `master2.py` with the `|z|^2 = 3` and `|z|^2 = 1` vector types added,
  1583 blocks / 245232 conflicts) did **not** close its bound. Its best answer at a 900 s
  limit is a 604-point configuration with maximum cosine exactly 1/2
  (`research/dim11-12/master_ell3.npy`), i.e. it re-finds the record and nothing better.
* The whole analysis assumes each 4-support contributes **all 16 sign patterns**. Dropping that
  is exactly the open case `n = 11` of Takhanov-Yun's signed-Johnson classification (they did
  `n <= 12`, `n != 11`). A vector-level search over a 9550-vector pool (all `+-2e_i`, all 5280
  signed 4-supports, 1344 `E_1`-type, 2688 `+-e_a+-e_b+-e_c+-u_j`, 192 `+-e_a +- sqrt3 w`,
  24 layer-only; conflict density 0.0246), seeded at the exact 604 and run for an hour from two
  seeds, never exceeded 604 (`research/dim11-12/mis11.py`).

### The layered picture, corrected

Along any base axis `e_i` the 604 splits as `386 + 2*108 + 2` with all three thresholds tight
(equator 1/2, heads 1/3, cross `1/sqrt3` exactly). The equator is **193 lines** and the head
code **54 lines**, both antipodal, with cosines in `(1/12)Z[sqrt2]` -- so the equator is *not*
a subcode of Ganzhinov's (rational) 510 and the `510 - d` model never applied to it.

**What the `510 - d` model does describe is Ganzhinov's own 592.** If the heads are taken
inside the 510-configuration `X` then every cross inner product is automatically `<= 1/2 <
1/sqrt3`, `d = h`, and the total is exactly `512 + h`. The dim-10 record is antipodal (255
lines), a 10-distance set with cosines `{0, +-1/6, +-1/4, +-1/3, +-1/2, +-1}`, and its
"cos > 1/3" graph is exactly the `cos = 1/2` graph (20670 edges; degrees 80 on 240 vertices and
82 on 270 -- Ganzhinov's `3*Phi_1 u i c Phi_2` split). CP-SAT proves its maximum independent
set is **exactly 80**, hence `512 + 80 = 592` -- Ganzhinov's dimension-11 value, and that route
is closed at 592.

### The head code: A(10, arccos 1/3) = 108 is also an exact ceiling

The 108 heads are the norm-3 vectors `+-1` on 12 triples (96) plus `+-e_6 +- sqrt2 u_j` (12).
In the same ansatz (triples on 10 points, plus `+-e_a +- sqrt2 w` vectors) the count is
`8|T| + 4*sum_a |J_a|`, where the `J_a` are *disjoint* subsets of the frame, so the second term
is at most `4*ell`. For `ell = 3` a pair-counting argument (the 9 layer pairs must split into
3 perfect matchings of a 6-set, whose complement then cannot be a perfect matching plus a
triangle) caps `|T|` at **12**, giving `8*12 + 12 = 108`. `ell = 2` gives at most 104 and
`ell = 4` at most 96. Literature check: Cohn's spherical-code table has **108 as the largest N
in dim 10 with cos <= 1/3** (0.333303602351); N=109 jumps to 0.335266 -- a clean gap, credited
only to "Henry Cohn, numerical", with no published construction. Upper bound 145 (Levenshtein
146, Boyvalenkov-Kazakov Example 6 removes 146). **Nothing above 108 exists anywhere.**

### Integral routes, all closed

* Pure integral, all-16-signs: `22 + 16*A(11,4,4) = 22 + 560 = 582`.
* **Integral scheme A at scale N = 16** (equator `(+-2^4,0^6)` on 4-sets, heads `(+-2^3,0^7)`
  on triples plus `+-2sqrt3 e_i`): the total is `16(q+t) + 4u + 2`; CP-SAT gives best 562 and
  an upper bound of **586 < 604** (`research/dim11-12/schemeA11.py`). The obstruction is sharp:
  `A(10,4,4) = 30` is attained by a **Steiner quadruple system SQS(10)**, which covers *every*
  triple, so a maximum equator leaves no head at all. Dimension 13 escapes this only because
  `R^12` contains norm-12 all-`+-1` vectors, which are compatible with the entire equator
  (`|sum_{i in S} +-2| <= 8`); `R^10` has no such vector. **That single fact is why scheme A
  gives 1154 in dimension 13 and at most 586 in dimension 11.**
* The lattice behind Ganzhinov: `L = span(510)/sqrt12` is a 10-dimensional **even** lattice,
  minimum 4, `det = 972 = 2^2 3^5`, shells `4:270, 6:960, 8:3240, 10:8640, 12:17790`; the 510
  is an `IP <= 6` subset of the norm-12 shell. Shell searches: norm-6 / `IP<=2` -> 60,
  norm-8 / `IP<=4` -> 270, norm-12 / `IP<=4` -> 56, norm-12 / `IP<=6` seeded at the 510 ->
  no improvement. None of these shells is a better source than the record itself.

### Dimension 12: the sqrt2-frame trick buys nothing

The same trade curve for `D = 12` (`research/dim11-12/trade.py 12 ell frame`):

| layer dim | k=0 | k=1 | k=2 | k=3 | k=4 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4 | 840 | 824 | 768 | 768 | 776 |
| 3 | 840 | 816 | 776 | - | - |
| 2 | 840 | 840 | 840 | - | - |

(all `k >= 1` rungs at layer dimension 4 are proved OPTIMAL). The maximum is always the
`k = 0` corner, `24 + 16*A(12,4,4) = 24 + 816 = 840` -- exactly Takhanov-Yun's classified
family. **The device that gains +22 in dimension 11 gains 0 in dimension 12**, because
`A(12,4,4) = 51` is realised by a Steiner-quadruple-type family whose blocks the transversality
condition destroys far faster than the `E_1` layer pays back.

### Why 605 is harder than 842 (contact statistics, recomputed)

| dim | n | contacts (cos > 0.4999) | dof `n(d-1) - d(d-1)/2` | ratio | rattlers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 10 | 510 | 20670 | 4545 | **4.55** | 0 |
| 11 | 604 | 19704 | 5985 | **3.29** | 0 |
| 12 | 841 | 9116 | 9185 | **0.99** | 28 |

Dimensions 10 and 11 are massively hyperstatic algebraic objects with no deformation
whatsoever; dimension 12's 841 is a critically jammed *numerical* packing. This matches the
numerical landscape exactly (605 at cos 0.5074 versus 842 at 0.5009) and says where effort
should go: dimension 11 will move only by a new algebraic idea, never by polishing.

### Files

`research/dim11-12/`: `load.py`, `build604.py` (exact `Q(sqrt2)` rebuild + verification),
`supports.py` (max |S| = 30, OPTIMAL), `trade.py` (the trade curves, D = 11 and 12),
`supk.py`, `master.py` / `master2.py` (general block ILPs), `pool11.py` / `mis11.py`
(9550-vector pool with partial sign patterns), `mis_head10.py` (the 80, OPTIMAL),
`anat11.py`, `raw10.py` / `shells10.py` / `shell_ls.py` (the Ganzhinov lattice), `rigid12.py`,
`schemeA11.py`. Logs in `research/dim11-12/out/`.


## 64. Both small-dimension records are MAXIMAL, and resist surgery (2026-08-21)

The published records in dimensions 11 and 12 were downloaded, rebuilt and tested directly.
Package `research/dim11-12/`: `maximal841.py`, `surgery.py`.

**Verification of the records themselves.** The dimension-12 841 was taken from
`k-nic/841_in_12D` (`gram841_coordinates.txt`): 841 unit vectors, maximum inner product
**0.499999937751**. The dimension-11 604 was rebuilt from its paper in exact Q(sqrt 2)
arithmetic: 604 vectors of norm 2, maximum inner product **exactly 1/2**.

**Neither admits an extra point.** A point z may be added iff
m(C) = min over unit z of max over x of <z,x> is at most 1/2; equivalently iff the maximum of
|z| over the polytope {z : <z,x> <= 1/2} is at least 1. Computed by ascent with an explicit
feasibility assertion on every returned point, and validated on a control configuration with a
known free direction (which returns |z| = 11.7):

| | max \|z\| | m(C) | margin over 1/2 |
|---|---|---|---|
| dimension 11, 604 points | 0.866025404 = sqrt(3)/2 | **1/sqrt(3) = 0.577350269** | 15.5% |
| dimension 12, 841 points | 0.818470192 | 0.610895797 | 22.2% |

The dimension-11 value is exact and structural: 1/sqrt(3) is precisely the constant that
governs that construction (an orthonormal frame of R^3 always has
max_j |(u_j)_k| >= 1/sqrt 3). Note also that both configurations turn out to be fully
antipodal -- every one of the 604 and all 841 points has a near-antipode -- correcting the
earlier report of 21 singletons in the 841.

**Neither admits surgery either.** Maximality only forbids insertion; surgery deletes D points
to admit Z > |D| new ones, which is how dimension 25 gained +2. Polishing 20 000 random
directions towards the deep holes and counting blockers B(z) = {x : <z,x> > 1/2}:

* dimension 11: the best direction has **3** blockers, and among all directions with at most 4
  no family of size 2, 3 or 4 has fewer blockers in total than its own size;
* dimension 12: the best has **4** blockers, same conclusion.

So both records are locally rigid in the strong sense, and any improvement needs a globally
different configuration -- consistent with the contact statistics (dimension 11 is hyperstatic
at ratio 3.29, dimension 12 critically jammed at 0.99).

**Correction to sections 11, 12, 30, 32 and 35.** Those model dimension 11 as
`510 - d + 2h + 2` with h an arccos(1/3)-code in R^10. That model does NOT describe the
604 record, whose equator has cosines in (1/12)Z[sqrt 2] and is not a subcode of Ganzhinov's
rational 510. What it does describe is Ganzhinov's own 592, and CP-SAT proves the maximum
arccos(1/3)-subcode of the 510 is exactly 80, giving 512 + 80 = 592. That route is closed, and
A(10, arccos(1/3)) = 108 is likewise the exact ceiling of its own ansatz.

**Provenance worth recording.** Dimension 11's 604 is Bianchi, Kwon, Pappu, Zou,
arXiv:2606.10402 -- a **cs.CL/cs.AI paper about an AI-agent platform**, which is why sweeps of
math.MG never surface it. The progression was 582 (Best 1980 / Zinoviev-Ericson 1999) -> 592
(Ganzhinov) -> 593 (AlphaEvolve) -> 594 -> 604. Dimension 10's 510 is Ganzhinov, *Highly
symmetric lines*, Linear Algebra Appl. 722 (2025) 12-37, a PSU(4,2) construction 270 + 3x80.
When hunting records in small dimensions, search outside math.MG.


## 65. Brainstorm on dimensions 11, 12, 19: what was tried and what it rules out

**(a) The cap construction run DOWNWARD is dead in both 11 and 12.** For an antipodal equator
in R^n, R^n (+) R^1 gives `N = |shell| + 2|C| + 2` with C a set of lines at pairwise
|cos| <= 1/3 (the k = 1 case of section 50). Measured:

| equator | lines | conflict degree (|cos| > 1/3) | best class | gives | needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 604 (dim 11) | 302 | 84 / 91.9 / 164 | 16 | 638 in dim 12 | 118 for 842 |
| 841 (dim 12) | 454 | 109 / 137.8 / 154 | 16 | 875 in dim 13 | 156 for 1155 |

The classes are an order of magnitude too small; these configurations are far too tight to
serve as equators. Incidentally the inner-product spectra confirm their natures: the 604 has
**22 distinct values** (exact, algebraic, in (1/12)Z[sqrt 2]) while the 841 has **10 348**
(a generic numerical packing).

**(b) A counting proof that the dimension-12 family caps at 840.** The 840 is
24 axis vectors (+-2e_i) plus weight-4 vectors (+-1,+-1,+-1,+-1,0^8) on supports S with sign
sets Sigma_S. Two supports meeting in THREE points would give inner product 3 > 2 unless
their restrictions to those coordinates are disjoint, so for every triple T

    sum over S containing T of |r_T(Sigma_S)|  <=  8.

Brute force over all 2^16 sign sets gives min sum over the four triples of |r_T| as a function
of |Sigma|: 4, 7, 10, 12, 15, 17, 19, 20, 23, 25, 27, 28, 30, 31, 32, 32 for m = 1..16. The
ratio (cost per vector) is **>= 2 always, with equality only at m = 16**, because ratio 2
forces Sigma closed under flipping every coordinate. Summing over all C(12,3) = 220 triples,

    sum_S |Sigma_S| <= 220*8/2 = 880.

But m = 16 forces r_T = 8 on all four triples, so those supports use their triples
exclusively -- they form a PACKING -- and any support with a non-empty sign set needs spare
capacity on all four of its triples. Once a maximum packing is in place no support can be
added, and A(12,4,4) = 51, giving 51*16 = 816 and the family total 24 + 816 = **840**.
Takhanov-Assylbekov-Yun's 841 is exactly one sphere beyond the ceiling of the entire family.
(A 3-(12,4,2) design, which would give 110*8 = 880, does not exist: lambda_1 = 36.67 is not an
integer.)

**(c) Dimension 19: coset structures are exhausted at exactly 320.** A complete search over
random subgroups M <= K of dimensions 3, 4, 5, 6, 7 with M n S = empty, solving each quotient
EXACTLY by CP-SAT, gives best totals of 256 in every dimension -- Cohn-Li's value. Ho's 320
comes from one very special M whose quotient is the Clebsch graph, and **320 is the maximum
over all coset structures**. Anything above 320 must be a non-coset independent set.

**(d) The connection set S is a SIDON SET.** No two elements sum to an element of S
(triangle-free) and no four sum to zero (no 4-cycles), so all C(21,2) = 210 pairwise sums are
distinct -- S is a Sidon set of size 21 in F_2^10, and the Cayley graph has girth exactly 5.
This is the right language for a literature search: Cayley graphs on F_2^n with Sidon
connection sets are exactly the girth->=5 ones, and are studied in connection with APN
functions. Maximum Sidon sets in F_2^10 have size about 32, so 21 is not extremal.


## 66. Dimension 13 fully decoded -- the last 1999 record in the low range (2026-08-21)

Dimension 13 is the ONE value in dimensions 9-16 still standing from Zinoviev-Ericson 1999
(reference [30] of Cohn's table). Everything around it moved recently: dimension 10 -> 510 and
dimension **14 -> 1932 by Ganzhinov (2025)**, dimension 11 -> 604 (2026, an AI-agent paper),
dimension 12 -> 841 (2026). That made it the obvious target. Package `research/dim13/`.

**Getting the configuration.** Cohn's `dimensions1-24.txt` (23.9 MB) is not reachable through
the DSpace web UI; it downloads from the REST API,
`https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/{uuid}/content`, with the uuid found via
`server/api/pid/find?id=hdl:1721.1/153312`. Dimension 13 needs irrational coordinates
(entries like `2*sqrt(3)`), so a naive parser silently drops 48 of the 1154 points.

**The decode.** At norm 16 the 1154 splits over its last coordinate as

| layer | x_13 | count | shape |
|---|---|---|---|
| poles | +-4 | 1 + 1 | (0^12, +-4) |
| caps | +-2 | 168 + 168 | 144 of ((+-1)^12, +-2) and 24 of (+-2sqrt3 e_i, +-2) |
| equator | 0 | 816 | (+-2^4, 0^8) |

816 + 336 + 2 = 1154, and **every ingredient is an optimal code**:

* the equator is 16 * A(12,4,4) = 16 * 51 = 816 -- exactly dimension 12's 840 family
  WITHOUT its 24 axis vectors;
* the sign caps are a binary code of length 12 and minimum distance 4 of size
  **144 = A(12,4) = A(11,3)**, which is the exact maximum (I first misremembered this as 256
  and it is worth stating plainly: A(11,3) = 144);
* the axis caps are 2 per coordinate.

**Why the axis vectors are dropped.** An axis point (+-4 e_i, 0) meets a cap (y, +-2) in
4 y_i, so it needs |y_i| <= 2. Exactly 4 caps violate this for each coordinate -- precisely
(+-2sqrt3 e_i, +-2). So per coordinate the choice is 2 axis points or 4 axis caps, and the
caps win. That is the whole reason dimension 13's equator is 816 and not 840.

**Everything is saturated.** Both cap conditions are attained exactly: cap-equator reaches
1/sqrt(3) = 0.577350269 (its limit) and cap-cap reaches 1/3 (its limit). The 144-word code
cannot be extended: **zero** sign vectors sit at Hamming distance >= 4 from all 144.

**Maximality.** m(C) = min over unit z of max <z,x> = **2/sqrt(13) = 0.554700196** exactly, so
no point can be added. The margin over 1/2 is **10.9%** -- the loosest of the three:

| dim | record | m(C) | margin | fewest blockers |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 11 | 604 | 1/sqrt(3) = 0.5774 | 15.5% | 3 |
| 12 | 841 | 0.6109 | 22.2% | 4 |
| **13** | **1154** | **2/sqrt(13) = 0.5547** | **10.9%** | **2**, and some directions violate only ONE constraint |

**A side-observation worth keeping.** The cap layer is a **168-point arccos(1/3) code in
R^12**, which is well above the figures recorded in section 30 for that quantity; those were
caps of record 60-degree codes, not the best arccos(1/3) codes. Anyone attacking
A(12, arccos 1/3) should start from this 168.

**What is still open here.** Directions exist violating exactly one constraint. A single
violation is worth nothing because the configuration is antipodal -- adding y at height +2
forces -y at height -2, and if y clashes with equator point x then -y clashes with -x, so two
deletions pay for two insertions. The profitable move is **two compatible directions sharing
one violated antipodal pair**: delete 2, insert 4, giving 1156. `pairswap.py` searches for
exactly that.


## 67. Every published record in dimensions 9-15 is MAXIMAL, with exact margins (2026-08-21)

Cohn's `dimensions1-24.txt` gives explicit coordinates, so the free-point test of section 45
can be run on the whole low range at once (`research/dim13/sweep.py`). Result: **none of them
admits an extra sphere**, and the minimax values are exact algebraic numbers.

| dim | record | source | m(C) | exact | margin over 1/2 | fewest blockers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 9 | 306 | | 0.577350269 | **1/sqrt(3)** | 15.5% | |
| 10 | 510 | Ganzhinov 2025 | 0.670820393 | | 34.2% | |
| 11 | 604 | AI agents 2026 | 0.577350269 | **1/sqrt(3)** | 15.5% | 3 |
| 12 | 841 | Takhanov et al. 2026 | 0.604964075 | | 21.0% | 4 |
| **13** | **1154** | Zinoviev-Ericson 1999 | 0.554700196 | **2/sqrt(13)** | **10.9%** | **2** |
| **14** | **1932** | Ganzhinov 2025 | 0.534522484 | **sqrt(2/7)** | **6.9%** | 3 |
| **15** | **2564** | Leech-Sloane **1971** | 0.534522484 | **sqrt(2/7)** | **6.9%** | 3 |

Dimensions 14 and 15 share the same minimax exactly, which points at a common structure. They
are also the tightest in the range -- their deep holes are only 6.9% too shallow -- so they,
not 11 or 12, are the natural surgery targets. Note also how old some of these are: dimension
15 is **Leech-Sloane 1971** and dimension 16 is Barnes-Wall 1959.

## 68. Layering over a record equator does not work, and here is the measurement

Section 66 showed the records are layered: `N = |E| + 2|L| + 2` with the caps at height 2
obeying `<x-hat,y-hat> <= 1/sqrt(3)` against the equator and `<y-hat,y'-hat> <= 1/3` among
themselves. In both decoded cases the equator is well BELOW the record of the dimension
underneath:

    dim 13: equator 816  against dimension 12's record 841   (25 short)
    dim 15: equator 1484 against dimension 14's record 1932  (448 short)

so the obvious move is to substitute the record. The cap region is non-empty exactly when
m(E) <= 1/sqrt(3) = 0.5774, and both records qualify (0.5547 and 0.5345) -- but the layer
collapses:

| target | equator | cap layer found | cap layer NEEDED | result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| dim 14 | dimension-13 record, 1154 | **23** | 389 | 1202 against 1932 |
| dim 15 | dimension-14 record, 1932 | **28** | 316 | 1990 against 2564 |

An order of magnitude short. The reason is exactly what sections 45 and 64 measured: a record
configuration is maximal with a small margin, so its 1/sqrt(3)-free region is a thin sliver
that holds only a couple of dozen directions at pairwise 1/3. The published constructions use
a DELIBERATELY sub-maximal equator -- 816 rather than 841 -- precisely to keep the cap region
big, and that trade is already optimised. Same conclusion as section 65(a), now measured on
the other side of the trade.

**The rule this yields:** in a layered kissing construction the equator should be chosen to
maximise `|E| + 2|L(E)|`, and `|L(E)|` falls off a cliff as `m(E)` approaches `1/sqrt(3)`.
Substituting a record equator is always a mistake.

## 69. The antipode construction IS a max-weight clique problem, and that caps it (2026-08-21)

Sun-Wang (arXiv:2607.20359v3) and Chen et al. (2025) state the antipode construction as: for a
lattice `L` of minimal norm `mu`, a rank-`k` cross-section with Gram `K`, and `M = K^{-1}`,
pick a finite `S ⊂ M` whose maximal pairwise squared distance `delta` is `< mu`.  Then

    N_i = sum over j with |u_i - u_j|^2 = delta  of  fibre(u_j - u_i),
    fibre(c) = #{ w in L : |w|^2 = mu, U(w) = c },      U(w) = (<u_1,w>, ..., <u_k,w>),

and the packing's kissing number is `max_i N_i`.  Chen et al.'s Theorem 2 takes `S` to be the
dual-basis simplex `{0, m_1, ..., m_k}`.  That is one point of a large search space.  The whole
space collapses to a single graph problem:

**Theorem.**  Over all admissible `S`,

    max_S max_i N_i  =  max over delta in (0, mu) of
                        MaxWeightClique( V_delta, E_delta, fibre )

where `V_delta = { v in M : |v|^2 = delta, fibre(v) > 0 }` and `v ~ v'` iff `<v,v'> >= delta/2`.

*Proof.*  (<=) Fix `S` and the index `i` attaining the max; let `v_j = u_j - u_i` run over the
diameter partners of `u_i`.  Each `|v_j|^2 = delta`, and for two partners
`|v_j - v_l|^2 = |u_j - u_l|^2 <= delta`; with `|v_j|^2 = |v_l|^2 = delta` that is exactly
`<v_j, v_l> >= delta/2`.  So `{v_j}` is a clique of weight `N_i`.
(>=) Given a clique `V`, set `S = {0} u V`.  Then `|v|^2 = delta` and
`|v - v'|^2 = 2delta - 2<v,v'> <= delta`, so the maximum pairwise distance is exactly `delta`,
every pair `(0,v)` is a diameter pair, and `N_0 = sum_{v in V} fibre(v)`.  Centring at `0` is
free because `M` is a lattice and only the difference set matters.  QED

**Corollary (the 30-degree cap ceiling).**  `<v,v'> >= delta/2` with `|v| = |v'| = sqrt(delta)`
says the angle between any two arms is at most 60 degrees, i.e. all arms lie in a cap of
angular radius 30 degrees.  So the antipode kissing number is at most the fibre mass of the
heaviest 30-degree cap of a single sphere `|v|^2 = delta` -- it can never reach even half the
shell.  In particular the antipode can beat the bare cross-section `fibre(0)` only when the
fibre distribution is very flat.

**Corollary (why the simplex is the worst case).**  If `delta` is the minimal norm of `M` then
`v - v'` is a nonzero `M`-vector of norm `<= delta`, hence `= delta`: the clique is equilateral,
its Gram is `(delta/2)(I + J)`, positive definite of rank `m`, so `m <= k`.  That is precisely
the dual-basis simplex regime.  Larger `delta` allows larger stars but lighter fibres.

**Measurement (`research/dim13/antipode3.py`, Leech, greedy cross-sections, k = 1..12).**  The
optimum is always attained at small `delta` with a 1-4 arm star, and always loses:

    dim 23  bare  93150   star  47104  (1 arm,  delta 1/4)     Lambda 93150   record 93150
    dim 22  bare  49896   star  41472  (2 arms, delta 1/3)     Lambda 49896   record 49896
    dim 20  bare  15540   star   7040  (2 arms, delta 3/5)     Lambda 17400   record 19448
    dim 18  bare   5820   star   4440  (4 arms, delta 11/15)   Lambda  7398   record  7654
    dim 16  bare   2640   star   2100  (4 arms, delta 4/5)     Lambda  4320   record  4320
    dim 12  bare    624   star    396  (3 arms, delta 1)       Lambda   648   record   841

So the antipode is a ~10% lever that only pays when the bare cross-section is already within
about 10% of the target -- which is the dimension-44-to-47 situation, not the low-dimensional
one, where the bare cross-section is 8-36% below the record.  **The antipode construction does
not produce low-dimensional records, and this is a structural obstruction, not a search
failure.**  Cf. [[kissing-antipode-cap-ceiling]].

Two side facts recorded while doing this:

* The chain `u_1, ..., u_k` must NOT be built from mutually orthogonal minimal vectors.  Only
  the cross-section has to be orthogonal to all of them; restricting the candidates to the
  surviving orthogonal set gives 43164 in dimension 22 instead of Lambda_22's 49896.  Greedy
  over the whole shell recovers the laminated values 93150 / 49896 / 27720 / 17400 / 10668 /
  7398 exactly (section 53's chain Z, A_2, A_3, D_4, D_5, E_6, E_7, E_8).
* The antipode packing is, concretely, `|S|` translates of the cross-section lattice
  `L' = L ∩ K^⊥`, at minimum distance `sqrt(mu - delta)`: `P = { pi(w) : w in L, U(w) in S }`,
  and `|pi(w) - pi(w')|^2 = |w - w'|^2 - |U(w) - U(w')|^2 >= mu - delta`.  Contacts inside one
  translate are at `sqrt(mu) > sqrt(mu - delta)` and therefore never count.  Getting them to
  count would need the translates to be `sqrt(mu)` apart mod `L'`, i.e. covering radius
  `>= sqrt(mu)`; the Leech's is `sqrt(2)`, so that door is shut.

## 70. Unions of fibres on a common sphere: a new construction, and its ceiling (2026-08-21)

Section 69's cap ceiling comes from the antipode leaving the projected points at their natural
radii, so that only contacts at the shrunken distance count.  Rescaling each fibre to a common
sphere instead gives a different -- and genuinely new -- construction.

Fix `k` Leech minimal vectors spanning a cross-section `K`, put `M = K^{-1}`, and label each
minimal vector `w` by `v = U(w)` in `M`.  Splitting `w = p(w) + pi(w)` with `p(w)` in
`span(K)`:

    |pi(w)|^2 = 4 - |v|^2 ,      <pi(w), pi(w')> = <w,w'> - <v,v'>  <=  2 - <v,v'>,

the bound because `w - w'` is a nonzero Leech vector, so `<w,w'> <= 2`.  Normalising every
fibre to the unit sphere, the union over a label set `W` has all pairwise cosines `<= 1/2` iff

    4 - 2<v,v'>  <=  sqrt( (4 - |v|^2)(4 - |v'|^2) )        for all v, v' in W,      (*)

and it then has `sum_{v in W} fibre(v)` points in dimension `24 - k`.  Taking `v = v'` gives
`4 - 2a <= 4 - a`, true always, so **every single fibre is already a valid kissing
configuration** -- (*) is exactly the compatibility of two of them.  Maximising is a
max-weight clique (`research/dim13/fibreunion.py`, exact rational edge test in `focus16.py`).

Three things make this NOT a repackaging of the bare cross-section:

* `(*)` fails whenever `v = 0` and `v' != 0` (it reduces to `b <= 0`), so the zero fibre is
  never part of a larger union.  The construction is disjoint from the laminated lattice.
* `(*)` does not require `|v| = |v'|`: fibres of DIFFERENT norms may be combined, which the
  antipode's star cannot do.
* It never produces a repeated point: `|v - v'|^2 >= 4` would force `4 - 2<v,v'> >= 8 - a - b`,
  and AM-GM gives `sqrt((4-a)(4-b)) <= (8-a-b)/2`, so `(*)` fails.

Validated against hand computation at `k = 1`: labels `c = 1` and `c = 2` are compatible
(`3 <= sqrt(11.25)`) and `c = 1, -1` are not (`4.5 > 3.75`), giving `47104 + 4600 = 51704`,
exactly what the solver returns.

**It works, and it still loses.**  With the laminated cross-sections recovered exactly
(93150 / 49896 / 27720 / 17400 / 10668 / 7398 / 5346 / 272):

    dim 23   bare 93150   union 51704      dim 18   bare  7398   union  6208
    dim 22   bare 49896   union 44288      dim 16   bare  4320   union  2574
    dim 21   bare 27720   union 22528      dim 12   bare   648   union   432
    dim 20   bare 17400   union 17920 <-- beats Lambda_20      dim  9  bare 272  union 210

Dimension 20 is the one win, and it is a real one -- 17920 > 17400 = Lambda_20 -- but it is
still short of the record 19448.  For `k >= 5` the union always loses to the bare fibre.

**Why, structurally.**  At equal norms `a = b`, (*) reduces to `<v,v'> >= a/2`, i.e. 60 degrees
-- the same 30-degree cap as section 69.  Mixed norms are *harder*, not easier: for
`a = 1, b = 3` the requirement is `cos >= 0.655`, about 49 degrees.  So the label set is
essentially a cone of half-angle 30 degrees (with radial freedom), and the construction is
capped by the fibre mass of the heaviest such cone.  The Leech's fibre mass is concentrated at
`v = 0`, which is precisely the label the construction may not use.

Checked on P48 as well, using the exact 2-point cells from the moment LP (section on
dimension 46): with `K = [[6,3],[3,6]]`, `{(1,0),(1,1)}` is compatible at exact equality and
gives `5365440 + 5365440 = 10730880`, against the bare `n[0,0] = 12309600`.  Adding `(2,1)`
reaches 11653065, still below.  So the same verdict holds for the extremal lattices.

## 71. Dimension 19: the Cayley graph decomposed, and what now decides it (2026-08-21)

`tau(19) = 10668 + 4*alpha(Cay(K,S))`, `K = F_2^10`, `|S| = 21` (section 31).  Ho has
`alpha = 320`; `subgroups.py` proved every coset-structured set tops out at exactly 320.  The
graph itself turns out to be highly structured, and the structure reduces the problem twice.

**What the graph is** (`research/dim19/spectrum.py`).  `S` is sum-free (no `s + s'` in `S`) and
a Sidon set (all pairwise sums distinct), so `Cay(K,S)` is 21-regular on 1024 vertices with
**girth exactly 5**.  Its maximum clique is therefore 2, and the clique-coclique bound gives
only `alpha <= 512` -- useless.  The full spectrum is

    21 x1,  19 x1,  11 x16,  9 x16,  5 x130,  3 x290,  1 x160,
    -3 x120,  -5 x200,  -7 x80,  -11 x5,  -13 x5

so the Hoffman ratio bound is `1024*13/34 = 391`, weaker than the Delsarte theta of 384.

**First reduction: eigenvalue 19 has multiplicity one.**  An eigenvalue `20 - 1` means a
character trivial on 20 elements of `S` and non-trivial on one, so 20 of the 21 lie in a
hyperplane `K_0` and exactly one, `t`, does not -- concretely `K_0` is the even-weight part,
`S` being one weight-3 word plus twenty weight-4 words.  Splitting `K = K_0 u (K_0 + t)`:
each half induces `G_0 = Cay(K_0, S_0)` (512 vertices, 20-regular, `S_0` = the twenty weight-4
words), and across the halves `x` and `y + t` are adjacent iff `x + y + t` is in `S`; since
`x + y` is in `K_0` and `t` is the only element of `S` outside it, this forces `x = y`.  **The
cross edges are a perfect matching.**  Hence

    alpha(Cay(K,S)) = max { |A| + |B| : A, B independent in G_0, disjoint }
                    = the maximum induced BIPARTITE subgraph of G_0.

**Second reduction: the five blocks share a sum.**  `G_0`'s spectrum is
`{20 x1, 10 x16, 4 x130, 2 x160, -4 x120, -6 x80, -12 x5}`.  The five characters at `-12` have
a common kernel `N` of dimension 5, and `K_0/N = F_2^4` carries the **Clebsch graph**, each `s`
in `S_0` mapping to one of its five generators, four of `S_0` per generator -- which is exactly
where Ho's `64 * alpha(Clebsch) = 64 * 5 = 320` comes from.  Each block of four is a Sidon set
whose six differences span a 3-dimensional space, and (`structure.py`) **all five blocks have
the same sum**

    z = g_1 + g_2 + g_3 + g_4 = 499,  the same element of N for every block,

so `z` lies in all five direction spaces.  `z` is in `N` and `S_0` misses `N`, so `z` is not in
`S_0`; nor is `z` a difference of two elements of `S_0` (within a block the six differences are
listed and exclude it; across blocks the class is nonzero).  So `<z>`-cosets are independent
and `G_0` is the `<z>`-double cover of `G_1 = Cay(K_0/<z>, S_0)`, 256 vertices, 20-regular.

**Exact answers on 256 vertices** (`quotient256.py`, CP-SAT, both OPTIMAL):

    alpha(G_1)  = 80        maxbip(G_1) = 160

Lifting full `<z>`-cosets gives `alpha(G_0) >= 160` and `maxbip(G_0) >= 320` -- Ho's record,
reproduced from scratch.  **And this family cannot do better**: `|A|, |B| <= alpha(G_1) = 80`
caps `maxbip(G_1)` at 160, hence the lift at exactly 320.

**What is left.**  `maxbip(G_0) <= 2*alpha(G_0)` and `alpha(G_0)` is in `[160, 192]` (the lower
from the lift, the upper from `G_0`'s ratio bound `512*12/32`).  So:

* `alpha(G_0) = 160`  =>  `maxbip(G_0) <= 320`  =>  **dimension 19 is closed at 11948**;
* `alpha(G_0) >= 161` is the only remaining door to a new record.

A maximum independent set of `G_0` need NOT be a union of `<z>`-cosets: writing `A_2` for the
fibres fully inside `A` and `A_1` for the singly-used ones, `|A| = 2|A_2| + |A_1|`, and because
`s + z` is never in `S_0`, adjacent vertices of `G_1[A_1]` can both be used provided their
lifts are chosen with opposite signs.  The condition is a voltage/tension condition on cycles,
so the question is genuinely open, not a formality.  `alpha512.py` attacks it with
vertex-transitivity (`x_0 = 1`), maximality, and girth-5 cuts (every 5-cycle carries at most
two chosen vertices).  Cf. [[kissing-dim19-open-door]].

### 70a. Dimension 16 measured over the RIGHT cross-section (2026-08-21)

Every dimension-16 number from the greedy chain was measured over the wrong lattice: at rank 8
the greedy returns cross-sections of 2772 or 2982 minimal vectors, not Lambda_16's 4320.
`research/dim13/e8section.py` builds the rank-8 sublattice to order instead -- eight Leech
minimal vectors whose Gram is twice the E_8 Cartan matrix (diagonal 4, `-2` on each Dynkin
edge, 0 elsewhere), found by backtracking with the inner products forced at each step.  It
comes out with `det = 256` and orthogonal complement of exactly **4320** minimal vectors, i.e.
genuine `sqrt(2) E_8` and `Lambda_16`.

The fibre union over it gives **4096 = 8 x 512**, against the record 4320.  The 8 is forced,
not incidental: at equal label norms the compatibility rule is `<v,v'> >= a/2`, so the labels
are roots pairwise at 60 degrees, whose Gram `(a/2)(I+J)` is positive definite of rank `m`,
giving `m <= 8` in a rank-8 `M`.  So the construction reaches 94.8% of Barnes-Wall and stops
there for a reason that can be written down in advance.

Adding the zero fibre to those eight is impossible label-wise, and impossible point-wise too:
a norm-1-label point conflicts with about `4600 * 4320/196560 = 101` points of `fibre(0)`, so
each point bought costs a hundred.  **Dimension 16 stays at 4320.**

## 72. DIMENSION 19 IS CLOSED: tau(19) = 11948 is exactly the ceiling (2026-08-21)

Section 31 left dimension 19 as the only open case of the Cohn-Li / Ho family, with Ho's
`alpha = 320` per component against a Delsarte-theta ceiling of 384 (section on the exact
ceilings: "19 | 5 | 21 | 4 x 1024 | 384.0000 | 1536 | 1281 | OPEN, room 256").  Raw CP-SAT on
the 1024-vertex component got 320 with a proven upper bound of only 348.  **It is now closed at
exactly Ho's value.**

The chain, each link either enumerated or solved to proven optimality:

1. `tau(19) = 10668 + 4*alpha(Cay(K,S))`; the Cayley graph on the punctured Golay dual has
   four components, each a translate of `Cay(K,S)` with `K = F_2^10`, `|S| = 21`.
2. `S` has exactly one odd-weight element `t` (the weight-3 word) and twenty even ones, so
   `K = K_0 u (K_0 + t)` with `K_0` the even-weight hyperplane.  Both halves induce
   `G_0 = Cay(K_0, S_0)` (512 vertices, 20-regular) and the cross edges are a **perfect
   matching** `x <-> x + t`, because `x + y` lies in `K_0` and `t` is the only element of `S`
   outside it.  Verified vertex by vertex in `research/dim19/verify19.py`.
3. Hence `alpha(Cay(K,S)) = max { |A| + |B| : A, B independent in G_0, disjoint }`, the maximum
   induced bipartite subgraph of `G_0`, which is at most `2*alpha(G_0)`.
4. **`alpha(G_0) = 160`, proven OPTIMAL** (`research/dim19/alpha512.py`).  The model is exact:
   `x_0 = 1` is free by vertex-transitivity, maximality holds for any *maximum* independent
   set, and each of the 40000 girth-5 cuts `sum over a 5-cycle <= 2` is valid because an
   independent set in `C_5` has at most two vertices.  **Cross-checked** by re-solving with the
   maximality constraint dropped (`check_nomax.log`): still 160, still OPTIMAL, so the closure
   does not rest on that link.  Without the girth-5 cuts the solver stalls at an upper bound of
   348, which is why the cuts -- not more time -- were the thing that mattered.
5. Therefore `alpha(Cay(K,S)) <= 320`, and Ho attains 320, so
   **`alpha = 320` exactly and `tau(19) = 10668 + 4*320 = 11948` is the exact ceiling of this
   construction.**

This tightens the ceiling from 12204 (Delsarte theta) and 12234 to the achieved 11948, and
retires the "OPEN, room 256" row.  **Every dimension from 17 to 23 in the Cohn-Li family is now
settled**: 18, 20, 21 by section 31's component arguments, 22 and 23 by the crude gap argument,
19 here; dimension 17 uses a different construction and is not comparable.

Two structural facts about `G_0` were what made it reachable, both recorded in section 71: the
five `-12`-eigenvalue characters give the Clebsch quotient (which is exactly where Ho's
`64 * 5 = 320` comes from), and all five 4-element blocks share the sum `z = 499`, making `G_0`
a `<z>`-double cover of a 256-vertex graph with `alpha = 80` and `maxbip = 160` -- so the lift
family reproduces 320 and provably cannot beat it.

**What did NOT work as an upper bound**, recorded so it is not retried: the clique-coclique
bound is useless (the graph is triangle-free, `omega = 2`, giving only 512); the Hoffman ratio
bound gives 391 on `Cay(K,S)` and 192 on `G_0`; and the Clebsch-class sumset relaxation
(`classlp.py`, with `f_i(m) = min |X + Ghat_i|` computed exactly for every `m`) returns only
256, because it permits every class to sit at 16 -- a profile the real problem forbids, since a
class at 16 would have to be `V_i`-periodic for all five labels at once, hence empty or all of
`N`.  Capturing that globally needs the periodicity, not just the cardinality.

### 67a. Dimensions 17, 18, 19 are maximal too (2026-08-21)

Section 67 established that every published record in dimensions 9-15 is maximal.  The same
test (`research/dim13/sweep.py`, `m(C) = min over unit z of max <z,x>`, a point may be added
iff `m(C) <= 1/2`) extends to the next three, using Cohn's own coordinates:

| dim | record | max \|z\| | m(C) | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 17 | 5730 | 0.957427108 | 0.522232968 | maximal, margin 4.4% |
| 18 | 7654 | 0.942809042 | 0.530330086 | maximal, margin 6.1% |
| 19 | 11948 | 0.957949816 | 0.521948010 | maximal, margin 4.4% |

All three have maximum inner product exactly 0.5, so they are valid 60-degree codes, and none
admits a free point.  So the dimension-25 trick (+2 by inserting into a gap) has no analogue
here, and dimensions 9-19 are now ALL known to be maximal.  Note this is maximality, not
optimality: a different configuration could still be larger -- but not one built by adding to
these.

## 73. Dimension 17 decoded, and the one place low dimensions still have room (2026-08-21)

`research/dim13/decode17.py` reads the 5730-point record out of Cohn's coordinates and finds
the layering axis by trying every point as an axis and keeping the one with fewest distinct
heights.  The answer is a **three-tier** configuration, not the two-tier one the arithmetic
`4320 + 2*704 + 2` suggests:

| height | -1 | -1/2 | -1/3 | 0 | +1/3 | +1/2 | +1 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| count | 1 | 512 | 192 | **4320** | 192 | 512 | 1 |

`4320 + 2*512 + 2*192 + 2 = 5730`.  The equator is Lambda_16 (maximum inner product exactly
0.5) and each `+-1/2` layer projects to a code of maximum inner product exactly `1/3`, which is
the correct cap condition at height 1/2.  `Lambda_17 = 4320 + 2*512 + 2 = 5346`, so **Cohn-Li's
+384 is exactly the two extra 192-layers at height +-1/3** -- a new tier, not a larger cap.
That is what section 22's remark "keeps everything and then gains 384" is describing.

The conditions on the `+-1/3` tier, derived from `sqrt(1-h^2) sqrt(1-h'^2) <y,y'> + h h' <= 1/2`:

    against itself      <y,y'> <= 7/16
    against the +-1/2 tier   <y,y'> <= 1/(3*sqrt(2/3)) = 0.40825
    against the equator      <x,y> <= 1/2 / sqrt(8/9)  = 0.53033
    against the pole         1/3 <= 1/2, free

**Ceiling.**  The Delsarte LP for the single-cap relaxation (`research/dim13/caplp.py`) gives
`A(16, 1/3) <= 752.0007`, and the tiny off-grid overshoot is repaired rigorously by replacing
`f` with `(f - eps)/(1 - eps)`.  This reproduces the published `(16, 752, 1/3)` parameter set
exactly -- which Bannai-Sloane-type integrality separately rules out, so `A(16,1/3) <= 751`.
That validates the LP code, but it is NOT the ceiling for this configuration, because the real
structure has two tiers interacting rather than one cap of 704.

**Status of dimension 17: still open, and it is now the ONLY open low dimension.**  The record
is maximal (section 67a: `m(C) = 0.5222`, margin 4.4%, no free point), so an improvement needs a
different configuration, not an insertion.  Dimensions 9-15 are maximal, 16 sits at Lambda_16
with the fibre union reaching only 4096 (section 70a), 18-21 are closed for the Cohn-Li lever
(sections 31 and 72), and 22-23 are dead by the section-22 gap argument.  The concrete open
question is whether the `+-1/3` tier can exceed 192 over a Lambda_16 equator, or whether a
four-tier arrangement beats the three-tier one.

## 74. Dimension 17 reduced to a single integer (2026-08-21)

Section 73 decoded the record as `4320 + 2*512 + 2*192 + 2 = 5730` with a Lambda_16 equator.
Putting it in the integer frame turns the whole dimension into one graph parameter.

**The frame.**  Scale the equator to norm^2 8, so it is BW_16 in standard coordinates: 3840
vectors of shape `1^8` supported on the 30 octads of RM(1,4) (128 signs each, one parity
class) and 480 of shape `2^2`.  Every other point of the configuration is
`(v, s)/|(v,s)|` with `v` in R^16; the pole at height 1 forces `h <= 1/2`, i.e. `3s^2 <= |v|^2`,
and the loosest admissible `s` is `|v|/sqrt3`.  All the layering inequalities then become small
integer bounds.  With Cohn-Li's `s = sqrt2`:

    e.e' <= 4     a.a' <= 2     b.b' <= 7     e.a <= 4     e.b <= 6     a.b <= 4

where tier A is `|v|^2 = 6` at height 1/2 and tier B is `|v|^2 = 16` at height 1/3.

**Only two tiers can exist.**  The `2^2` equator vectors give `v.e = +-2v_i +- 2v_j`, so the two
largest entries obey `2(|v_i| + |v_j|) <= B = (2sqrt2/sqrt3)|v|`; an entry of size 2 needs
`|v|^2 >= 14`, and at 14 or 16 the octad constraint then fails.  So every tier is a shape `1^k`,
and for a support `T` the octad bound is `|T n O| <= floor(B)` while `|T n O| + |T n O^c| = k`.
Enumerated exhaustively:

| k | 4 | 5 | 6 | 7 | 8 | 9 | 10 | 11-15 | 16 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| valid supports | 0 | 0 | **448** | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | tier B |

**Superseded in part -- see section 74i**: this pass tested shapes `1^k` exhaustively but
shapes containing a 2 only up to `|v|^2 = 16`, and it missed `(2, 1^15)` at `|v|^2 = 19` and
`(3, 1^15)` at 24, both of which ARE valid tiers.  They turn out to be unusable (they exclude a
full tier A and the exchange rate against it is 1 gained to 16 lost), so the conclusion below
survives, but "forced" should read "the only USABLE shapes".

`k = 7` and `k = 8` are the interesting failures and both die by Fourier: `k = 7` needs
`|T n O| in {3,4}` for all 30 octads, so `|1_T-hat(f)| = 1` at every nonzero character, which
Parseval contradicts (15 against the required 63); `k = 8` needs `|T n O| = 4` exactly for all
30, forcing `1_T-hat` to vanish off zero, i.e. `1_T` constant.  **So the tier structure is
forced to be exactly Cohn-Li's -- there is no unused tier.**

**Tier A is exactly 512, and that is proven.**  Per support the signs form a length-6 code of
minimum distance 2, so at most `2^5 = 32`, attained by a parity class; and since all 32 are
present, any alignment on an intersection is realisable, so distinct supports need
`|S n S'| <= 2`.  Maximum clique over the 448 balanced 6-sets under that rule is **16, OPTIMAL**
(`research/dim13/supports17.py`), so `|A| = 32 * 16 = 512` and
`4320 + 2*512 + 2 = 5346 = Lambda_17` exactly -- Cohn-Li's `+384` is entirely tier B.
The bound is structural: each RM(1,4)-coset contains exactly 16 weight-6 vectors, and a family
is precisely one such coset (all its members give the SAME functional, because `|S n S'| = 2`
makes `1_S + 1_S'` an octad).  There are 28 families and all give isomorphic graphs.

**Tier B is one independence number.**  `e.b <= 6` forces `b`'s sign pattern `beta` into
`RM(1,4)^perp = RM(2,4)`, the [16,11,4] extended Hamming code; `a.b <= 4` adds one parity
condition per support, of RANK ONE, cutting `RM(2,4)` to a 1024-word coset `K'`; and
`b.b' <= 7` means Hamming distance `>= 5`, hence `>= 6` since all weights are even.  So

    |B| = alpha( Cay(K', W_4) ),   W_4 = the 60 weight-4 words of K',

a 1024-vertex 60-regular vertex-transitive graph with `omega = 4` and `lambda_min = -20`, so
clique-coclique and the Hoffman ratio bound both give `alpha <= 256`, and `A(16,6) = 256`
independently.  `RM(1,4)` sits inside `K'` and contains no weight-4 word, so its 32 cosets are
each INDEPENDENT and the graph is a blow-up over a 15-regular quotient on 32 vertices with
`alpha = 6` -- giving the coset-structured `32 * 6 = 192`, exactly Cohn-Li's tier B.  This is
the same shape of object as dimension 19's `G_0` (section 71), where the coset value turned out
to be optimal.

**Scope, stated precisely.**  Everything above is conditional on the configuration being
LAYERED over a `Lambda_16` equator with an antipodal pole pair, and on the tier vectors being
integral in the BW frame.  That family contains Cohn-Li's record and its natural
generalisations, and inside it the structure is forced.  It is NOT a proof that
`tau(17) <= 5734` in general: a configuration with no antipodal pair, or with many heights and
no 4320-point equator, is outside the analysis.  Half-integral tier shapes were checked by hand
(`(3/2, (1/2)^15)` and `1^4 (1/2)^8` both fail the octad bound) but not exhaustively.

**So dimension 17 is decided by one integer:**

    alpha >= 193  =>  tau(17) >= 5346 + 2*alpha >= 5732, a new record
    alpha  = 192  =>  the layered family over Lambda_16 is closed at 5730.

CP-SAT reaches 192 with an upper bound of 194 quickly, so the whole dimension now rests on a
gap of two.

### 74a. The heights are forced too, and the local structure of tier B (2026-08-21)

Section 74 fixed the tier SHAPES; the heights are forced as well.  Writing a tier as
`(v, s)/|(v,s)|`, the pole gives `3s^2 <= |v|^2` and the equator gives
`v.e <= sqrt2 sqrt(|v|^2 + s^2)`, which grows with `s`, so `s` wants to be as large as the pole
allows -- but the internal and cross-tier constraints tighten with `s`.  The outcome:

* **tier A (k = 6)**: the pole caps `s^2 <= 2`.  At `s^2 = 1` the equator bound is
  `sqrt2 sqrt7 = 3.74`, so `|T n O| <= 3` with `|T n O| + |T n O^c| = 6` forces `|T n O| = 3`
  for all 30 octads, i.e. `1_T-hat` vanishes off zero -- impossible.  So `s^2 = 2` exactly, and
  the height is exactly **1/2**.
* **tier B (k = 16)**: `s^2` may range over `[2, 16/3]` (below 2 the equator bound drops under
  6 and the parity trick dies; above 16/3 the pole does).  The internal bound
  `v.v' <= 8 - s^2/2` gives Hamming distance `>= 6` throughout, so nothing is gained there, but
  the cross-tier bound `v.a <= sqrt2(sqrt(16+s^2) - s)` is DECREASING in `s`: at `s^2 = 2` it is
  exactly 4 (disagreement `>= 1`), at `s^2 = 4` it is 3.496 (disagreement `>= 2`).  So `s^2 = 2`
  is optimal and the height is exactly **1/3**.

Checked at a non-pole height too: dropping the poles to allow `h = 0.6` still admits only
`k = 6` and `k = 16` (`k = 8, 9, 10, 12` all die by the same Parseval count), and it makes tier
B's internal requirement distance `>= 8`, where `A(16,8) = 32` -- far worse than 192.

**So Cohn-Li's configuration is the unique optimum of its family, up to tier B's independence
number.**

**Local structure of tier B's graph.**  `G = Cay(K', W_4)` is a blow-up of 32 fibres of 32 over
a quotient `Q` that is 15-regular on 32 vertices with `alpha(Q) = 6` and `omega(Q) = 4`.  The
induced independence numbers on unions of mutually adjacent fibres are strikingly rigid --
computed exactly:

| fibres (mutually adjacent) | 2 | 3 | 4 |
|---|---|---|---|
| vertices | 64 | 96 | 128 |
| independence number | **32** | **32** | **32** |

A second, third or fourth mutually adjacent fibre adds NOTHING.  These are valid cuts on the
fibre counts `a_c` that the vertex model cannot see (pairwise vertex constraints only give
`a_c + a_d <= 64`).  They are not sufficient on their own: `Q`'s minimum clique cover is 8
(eight disjoint 4-cliques, OPTIMAL), so the clique-cover bound is `32 * 8 = 256`, and closing
the last stretch to 192 needs the solver.  Recorded so the analytic route is not retried: the
fibre-clique family cannot get below 256 because `omega(Q) = 4` and `32/4 = 8 > 6`.

### 74b. Cross-sections through a degenerate equator (2026-08-21)

A hyperplane section of a kissing configuration is again a kissing configuration, so cross
sections of the dimension-18 record are candidate dimension-17 configurations.  Scanning every
configuration point as an axis gives a largest equator of only 4320 -- but that equator has
**rank 16, not 17**: it is `Lambda_16` sitting inside the 17-dimensional hyperplane, with the
tiers (rank 17) using the spare direction.  The dimension-18 record decodes as

    1 + 514 + 128 + 1024 + 4320 + 1024 + 128 + 514 + 1 = 7654,

heights `+-1, +-1/2, +-1/3, +-1/4, 0`, i.e. it is ALSO built over `Lambda_16`, just with two
spare dimensions instead of one -- which is why it can carry a `+-1/4` tier that dimension 17
provably cannot (section 74).

Degeneracy gives a whole circle of candidate sections: the equator's orthogonal complement in
R^18 is 2-dimensional, spanned by the axis `u` and one further direction `w`, so every
`w_theta = cos(theta) u + sin(theta) w` has a hyperplane containing the entire 4320-point
equator plus whatever tier points are orthogonal to it.  The count is piecewise constant and
jumps exactly where some point becomes orthogonal, so it is evaluated at the EXACT critical
angles (`tan theta = -(x.u)/(x.w)`, with `x.w = 0` giving `theta = pi/2` -- getting that case
wrong silently reports 5346 instead of 5602).

    best section over the circle: 5602        record: 5730

So it is a genuine dimension-17 configuration and a genuinely different construction, but 128
short.  Dimension 19's record has a rank-18 equator -- not degenerate -- so the trick is
specific to dimension 18 and does not repeat there.

### 74c. What else was tried on dimension 17, and why each is closed (2026-08-21)

Recorded so none of it is retried.

* **Extending the 5602 slice.** A cross-section of a maximal configuration need not be maximal
  -- 2052 points were discarded making it -- so the slice was tested for free points.  It is
  **maximal**, and with `m(C) = 0.522232968`, *exactly* the value of the dimension-17 record
  itself (section 67a).  Both therefore contain the same tight substructure, and no amount of
  greedy augmentation will move either.
* **Degenerate equators elsewhere.** The dimension-18 record's equator has rank 16, giving the
  circle of sections that produced 5602.  The dimension 19, 20 and 21 records have equators of
  rank 18, 19 and 20 -- full -- so there is no spare direction and the trick does not repeat.
* **Weakening the equator to open new tiers.**  Dropping the 480 `2^2` vectors admits a tier of
  `2^2`-shaped vectors, but its internal bound is `v.v' <= 1`, and since all inner products
  among `2^2` vectors are in `{0, +-4, +-8}` this forces `v.v' <= 0`; using all four sign
  patterns on a pair blocks every other pair meeting it, so the tier is a matching -- 32 points,
  against a cost of 480.  Dropping one octad's 128 equator vectors admits 7-supports, but
  Parseval then forces `T` to lie inside that octad, so `|T n T'| >= 6` and only ONE support is
  usable: 32 points against a cost of 128.  Both trades lose.
* **Partial sign classes in tier A.**  The 32 signs per support must be a parity class: they
  form an independent set of size 32 in the 6-cube, whose only maximum independent sets are the
  two parity classes.  And weakening one support gains nothing, because all 16 support
  functionals coincide on `RM(2,4)` -- a word excluded by one is still blocked by the other 15.
  Weakening ALL 16 at once costs 32 and admits at most a handful of words.
* **Fewer supports.**  Since the 16 functionals coincide, tier B's admissible slice is the SAME
  1024-word coset for every `m >= 1`.  So the total is `192 + 32m`, maximised at `m = 16`
  (704); `m = 0` frees tier B to the full `RM(2,4)` where the maximum distance-6 code is
  `A(16,6) = 256`, but the total is then only 256.  `m = 16` wins outright.
* **Fibre-level bounds.**  The local independence numbers are exactly 32 on 2, 3 and 4 mutually
  adjacent fibres, 64 on any two of the eight disjoint 4-clique blocks (all 28 pairs, exact),
  and 64 on every induced 5-hole.  Clique cuts, block-pair cuts and odd-hole cuts together give
  an ILP bound of exactly **256** and cannot do better, because `omega(Q) = 4` forces a clique
  cover of 8 and `32 * 8 = 256`.  Only the vertex-level solver can separate 192 from 194.

### 74d. The equator cannot be weakened either -- so dimension 17 is exactly one number

The last freedom was to shrink the equator: each octad's 128 vectors impose the constraint
`|S n O| <= 4` on tier A and the parity condition on tier B, so dropping one relaxes BOTH.
Priced exactly:

| octads dropped | equator | valid 6-supports | max family m' | best conceivable total |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 4320 | 448 | **16** (OPTIMAL) | 4320 + 2(512 + \|B\|) + 2 |
| 1 | 4192 | 476 | **16** (OPTIMAL) | 5730 even at \|B\| = 256 |
| 2 | 4064 | 504 | **16** (OPTIMAL) | 5602 even at \|B\| = 256 |

Relaxing the octad constraint admits more balanced 6-sets (448 -> 476 -> 504) but **not a larger
family**: the maximum under `|S n S'| <= 2` stays 16, proven optimal each time.  So tier A stays
at 512 while the equator shrinks by 128 per octad, and even granting tier B the absolute ceiling
`A(16,6) = 256` the total never exceeds 5730.  **The equator must be the full Lambda_16.**

Putting sections 74, 74a-74d together, dimension 17 is now a single integer with a two-sided
bracket:

    tau(17) = 5346 + 2 * alpha(Cay(K', W_4)),     192 <= alpha <= 256,

everything else -- equator, tier shapes, tier heights, tier A's size -- being forced and proven
optimal.  `alpha = 192` closes the dimension at 5730; `alpha >= 193` is a new record, and the
construction could in principle reach 5858.

### 74e. Dimension 17: the full-fibre case is closed at 192 (2026-08-21)

`tier B = alpha(Cay(K', W_4))`, and the graph is 32 fibres of 32 (each independent) over a
15-regular quotient `Q` with `alpha(Q) = 6`.  Split by whether any fibre is FULL.

**If some fibre `c` has `a_c = 32`, then `alpha <= 192`.**  Two adjacent fibres obey
`a_c + a_d <= 32` (the exact local bound on an edge of fibres, section 74a), so every one of
`c`'s 15 neighbours is empty and the whole set lives on `fibre(c) u R(c)` with
`R(c) = V \ N[c]`, 16 fibres.  Hence

    |A| <= 32 + alpha(G[R(c)]) = 32 + 160 = 192,

where `alpha(G[R(c)]) = 160` on those 512 vertices is computed **exactly** (CP-SAT, proven
optimal) in `research/dim13/decompose.py`.  `Q` is vertex-transitive, so the single computation
covers every `c`.  Sampling larger full-fibre sets confirms the pattern -- for `|I| = k` the
leftover has exactly `alpha(G[R(I)]) = 32(6-k)`, all exact -- so every case lands on 192:

| \|I\| | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| fibres left in R | 16 | 8 | 4 | 2 | clique | 0 |
| alpha(G[R]) | 160 | 128 | 96 | 64 | <= 32 | 0 |
| total | **192** | **192** | **192** | **192** | **192** | **192** |

(`|I| = 5` needs no computation: `alpha(Q[R]) <= 6 - 5 = 1` makes `R` a clique, and a clique of
fibres has local bound exactly 32.)

**So a 193-set would have to have NO full fibre at all** -- every `a_c <= 31`.  That is a
sharply more constrained search than the original, and it is the only case left between
`tau(17) = 5730` (closed) and a new record.

### 74f. Two reductions that bracket the last dimension-17 case (2026-08-21)

After section 74e a 193-set must have no full fibre.  Two further reductions squeeze the
largest fibre count `M = max_c a_c` from both ends.

**`M >= 29` is impossible.**  A neighbour `d` of `c` can only use points avoiding
`A_c + G_i`, and `y` lies outside `A_c + G_i` exactly when the 4-set `y + G_i` fits inside the
complement of `A_c`.  If `|A_c| >= 29` that complement has fewer than 4 elements, so no
translate fits, `A_c + G_i` is all of `F_2^5`, and EVERY neighbour of `c` is empty.  The set
then lives on `fibre(c) u R(c)` and

    |A| <= 29 + alpha(G[R(c)]) = 29 + 160 = 189 < 193.

(At `M = 28` the complement is a single 4-set, so it can be a translate of `G_i` for at most a
few `i`, and those neighbours are capped at 1 -- tight but not yet zero.)

**`M <= 6` is impossible.**  `Q` has a minimum clique cover by 8 disjoint 4-cliques (OPTIMAL),
and each 4-clique of fibres has exact local bound 32, so with every `a_c <= M`

    |A| <= 8 * min(32, 4M) = 8 * 24 = 192   for M <= 6.

So `7 <= M <= 28`, and vertex-transitivity puts the largest fibre at fibre 0.  That turns the
remaining question into 22 tight feasibility problems (`a_0 = M`, `a_c <= M`, 4-clique cuts,
`sum >= 193`), run in `research/dim13/finish17.py`.

**Also recorded: the blow-up identity holds on every subgraph tested.**  On cliques of 2, 3 and
4 fibres, on induced 5-holes, on pairs of blocks, on `R(I)` for `|I| = 1..4`, and on `N[c]`,
the answer is always exactly

    alpha(G[F]) = 32 * alpha(Q[F]).

`alpha(G[N[c]]) = 160 = 32 * 5` and `alpha(G[R(c)]) = 160 = 32 * 5` are both exact.  The
identity at `F = V` would give `32 * 6 = 192`.  It is NOT a theorem in general -- and the LP
cannot supply it here, since `omega(Q) = 4` forces the clique-cover bound to 256 -- so the last
case has to be settled by search.

### 74g. The exact cap on a 193-set's largest fibre (2026-08-21)

Section 74f gave `a_c >= 29 => at most 189` from a counting argument.  The same argument
sharpens into an exact bound.  A neighbour `d` of `c` can only use points `y` with
`y + G_i` inside the complement of `A_c`, so

    sum over the 15 neighbours of a_d  <=  P(t) := max over t-sets C of
                                            #{ block translates contained in C },  t = 32 - a_c,

and the total is at most `a_c + P(32 - a_c) + alpha(G[R(c)]) = a_c + P + 160`.  `P(t)` is a tiny
ILP (32 set variables, 480 translate variables) and comes out exact:

| t | 4 | 5 | 6 | 7 | 8 | 9 | 10 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| a_c | 28 | 27 | 26 | 25 | 24 | 23 | 22 |
| P(t) | 1 | 1 | 2 | 4 | 8 | 8 | 12 |
| a_c + P + 160 | 189 | 188 | 188 | 189 | **192** | 191 | 194 |

So every `a_c >= 23` is excluded, and **any 193-set has every `a_c <= 22`**.

The counting rests on a fact worth recording: the 15 blocks, translated into `F_2^5` so each
contains 0, lie in **15 DISTINCT translation classes**, and all `15 * 32 = 480` translates are
distinct.  That is why a 4-element complement can contain at most ONE block translate.

This is the tightest rigorous cap available from local counting.  It does not by itself close
the problem -- `max a_c <= 22` still permits `8 * 32 = 256` from the block cover, and the clean
proof would need `max a_c <= 6` -- but it removes the whole high-`a_c` regime from the search,
which is what the final solver runs use.

### 74h. Why the capped models answer the record question correctly

The final runs maximise `sum(x)` subject to every `a_c <= 22` (or `<= 28`), plus maximality.
Maximality is not valid for an arbitrary capped set -- a capped set need not be maximal in `G`
-- so the logic needs stating:

* if `alpha >= 193`, then a MAXIMUM independent set `A` has `|A| >= 193`, is maximal in `G`
  (being maximum), and has every `a_c <= 22` by section 74g.  So `A` satisfies every constraint
  of the capped model, and the model's optimum is at least 193;
* conversely, if the capped model's optimum is below 193, no independent set of size 193 or more
  exists at all, so `alpha = 192`.

So the capped optimum decides the question in both directions even though it is not itself
`alpha`.  Note also that the cap EXCLUDES Cohn-Li's own 192 (six full fibres), which is why the
capped runs sit far below it -- their incumbents are the best "spread" configurations, a
genuinely different part of the search space.

### 74i. Correction: the tier shapes are NOT quite forced -- but the extra ones are worthless

Section 74's classification was incomplete.  It tested shapes `1^k` exhaustively and shapes
containing a 2 only up to `|v|^2 = 16`, concluding that only `k = 6` and `k = 16` survive.  An
entry of size 2 needs `|v|^2 >= 14`, and larger norms were never checked.  Redone exhaustively
over all integer shapes with entries in `{0,1,2,3}` up to `|v|^2 = 40`
(`research/dim13/allshapes.py`), the valid tiers are

| \|v\|^2 | 6 | 16 | 19 | 24 | 24 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| shape | `1^6` | `1^16` | **`2 1^15`** | `2^6` | **`3 1^15`** |
| | tier A | tier B | new | = 2 x tier A, same directions | new |
