`tau(62) >= 2^26 = 67 108 864` and `tau(63) >= 2^27 = 134 217 728` from `[62,26,16]` and
`[63,27,16]`, both shortenings of one `[64,28,16]` (codetables.de, re-read 2026-08-23; the
construction chain is recorded in the package). `ceil(62/4) = ceil(63/4) = 16`, so the sign
vectors of those codes are kissing configurations outright. The mechanism is verified end to
end on three codes exhibited in coordinates -- extended Golay `[24,12,8]`, RM(1,5) `[32,6,16]`
and RM(2,5) `[32,16,8]`, the last with `d = ceil(32/4)` holding with equality so the boundary is
exercised -- with a negative control at `d = 15` that must and does fail. Only the two code
parameters are cited, which is the standing `A(38,10)` and `A(39,10)` already have in
`dim39-ers-constant-weight`. Both dimensions carry the status `external`: they double the moment
`[62,27,16]` or `[63,28,16]` is found.

`audit.py`'s `add` now keeps the LARGER of two claims for the same dimension and reports the
displaced one, so a second construction reaching a dimension can no longer win merely by being
called second. **The general trap, restated:** a lower bound on a rival construction sitting
below your value proves nothing. Only an upper bound on the rival does -- which is what
`ceiling.py` supplies, and why these two dimensions are now settled rather than merely flagged.

**CORRECTED 2026-08-25, and by the very mistake this section is about.** Dimensions 62 and 63
are not claimed at level 0. The full chain `(n, 15, 2)` reaches **71 310 732** and
**138 419 844**, against the `2^26` and `2^27` recorded above, and the package is now
`dim62-63-ers-chain` with `scripts/verify_chain.py`. Read the paragraph above again: it says
that a lower bound on a rival proves nothing and only an upper bound settles it, and then it
does exactly the wrong thing -- it bounds the CAP construction from above, finds level 0 of ERS
higher, and claims level 0. Level 0 is one term of a sum. Bounding one rival and evaluating one
term of another is not the same as bounding the second rival, and the ceiling argument that is
correct for the caps says nothing at all about how far ERS goes.

The audit now closes this. Step 4b used to gate on the level-0 floor; it gates on the full chain
instead, importing `best_chain` from `verifications/closed/dim96-ers-takeover/ers_exposure.py`
rather than keeping a second copy of the code tables -- a duplicated table is what had drifted
in three other places. Every chain input is a construction, so the floor is a LOWER bound on
ERS: a claim below it is definitely not an improvement, a claim above it is not thereby safe,
and the failure message says so. Level 0 survives only as a cross-check, since it is a term of
the chain and `best_chain` must never come out below it. Coverage is all 47 claims, and four
of them -- 39, 62, 63 and 96 -- ARE the chain, so they EQUAL the floor rather than exceed it;
4b has an equality branch for exactly that.

Dimension 39 read 428 437 here until 2026-08-26, and the gap was put down to the sweep carrying
older constant-weight tables than the package. **That explanation was wrong.** The tables were
never the weak part: `ers_exposure.sign0` read tables of LINEAR codes covering lengths 48..96
and returned 1 for everything below, so the chain's LARGEST term was being dropped in silence.
With sign0 falling back to the binary code table -- which also admits nonlinear codes, legitimate
for a sign code -- dimension 39 reads 756 116, exactly the claim, and `best_chain` now meets or
exceeds all six published ERS totals it can reach. Nothing in 49..96 moved. The lesson is the
one this file keeps relearning: a ratio tells you THAT something is wrong and never WHAT, and
the plausible cause ended the investigation for a day.

**The rule that survives all this**: before claiming a value, evaluate every rival construction
in FULL, not in the first term that happens to be easy to compute. `ers_exposure.py` is where
that is now done, and dimension 68 is the most exposed claim in the repository at a factor 1.47.


## 96. A debugging pass: two defects, two dimensions raised, and where the exposure is (2026-08-25)

Nothing in `run_all.py` was failing when this started; all 42 scripts passed. What follows is
what a pass looking for what the suite does NOT check turned up.

### The Edel-Rains-Sloane floor was taken at level 0 only -- the same mistake, one level up

Section 95 moved dimensions 62 and 63 onto `A(n_0, ceil(n_0/4))`, the single-support term, and
called it "level 0 of Edel-Rains-Sloane". It is level 0, and level 0 is not the construction.
The chain

    N(n) = sum_nu A(n, n_nu, n_nu) * A(n_nu, ceil(n_nu/4)),   n >= n_0 >= 4 n_1 >= 16 n_2 ...

is what carries ERS's own dimension-64 total from `2^28 = 268 435 456` to `331 737 984`, a
factor 1.24, and `ers_sweep.py` never looked at it. Evaluated at 62 and 63 with the chain
`(n, 15, 2)`:

| dim | level 0 | level 1 | level 2 | total | was |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 62 | 2^26 = 67 108 864 | 4096 x 1024 | 1891 x 4 | **71 310 732** | 67 108 864 |
| 63 | 2^27 = 134 217 728 | 4096 x 1024 | 1953 x 4 | **138 419 844** | 134 217 728 |

**Levels 1 and 2 are built, not cited.** Level 1 needs 4096 supports of weight 15 meeting
pairwise in at most 7: a **cyclic [15,6,8]_4** with defining set `{0,1,2,3,4,5,8,10,12}` (a
union of cyclotomic cosets mod 15 under `x -> 4x`, minimum distance 8 by enumerating all 4^6
codewords) pushed through the **grid map** -- index a 15 x 4 grid, one cell per row, two
supports meet in exactly `15 - d_H`. All 8 386 560 overlaps checked. The signs are a
`[15,10,4]`, the extended Hamming `[16,11,4]` shortened once. A brute-force sweep of all cyclic
GF(4) codes of length 15 reproduces codetables.de's entire best-known row (d = 15, 12, 11, 10,
8, 8, 7, 6 for k = 1..8), which is a free cross-check on that table.

**The whole chain is checked in coordinates where the answer is known**: `n = 8`, chain (8,2),
gives 240 points -- the E_8 root system -- and `n = 16`, chain (16,4,1), gives 4320, the
Barnes-Wall value and the record. Every pair of both. Landing on 240 and 4320 exactly, from a
construction that knows nothing of either lattice, is what licenses running it at 62 and 63.

`ers_exposure.py` now runs the full chain in EVERY claimed dimension and ranks each claim by
how far the best published level-1 support code would have to be beaten to overturn it.

### Dimension 68 is the most exposed claim in the repository

Its floor is quoted as 331 737 984, which is ERS's **dimension-64** total inherited by
monotonicity -- not the value of their construction **in dimension 68**. The same chain
`(64,16,4,1)` has four more coordinates there:

    ERS(68) >= 2^28 + 30828*2048 + A(68,4,4)*16 + 136 = 331 771 800

with `A(68,4,4) = C(68,3)/4 = 12529` exactly (68 = 2 mod 6, so an S(3,4,68) exists). The claim
clears that by **1.089**, so:

> **dimension 68 falls to any constant-weight code with A(68,16,16) >= 45 235.**

The best in hand is ERS's own 30 828, monotone in n from their length-64 extended cyclic code:
a factor **1.47**, against Gilbert-Varshamov and Johnson growth rates from 64 to 68 of 1.67 and
1.79, and against ERS's own code beating the generic grid map by 1.88 at the same parameters.
Dimensions 70 and 71 additionally clear a *generous* ERS bound (563 125 646, handing ERS the
length-80 constant-weight code); dimension 68 does not. Ranked exposure: **68 at 1.47, 95 at
3.9, everything else above 5**. What protects it is that nobody has published an A(68,16,16).

### The GPU class certificate is not reproducible across software stacks

`gpu/run.py` claimed to be "bit-identical on any machine". Measured: the 31 000-class
`cert_prod.json` made 2026-08-22/23 did **not** regenerate under torch 2.11.0+cu128 -- the
first 300 classes came back summing to 595 325 against the certificate's 601 370.
`diag_repro.py` separates four hypotheses and settles three: the pool IS reproducible
in-process (identical SHA-256), `ip_histogram` is inert, writing the state is inert. What moved
is the environment. Every random *decision* is numpy's, but the pool is *assembled* by torch
primitives whose tie-breaking is unspecified (`dedup_into` sorts by a 64-bit key), and the
pool's ROW ORDER is what the greedy window is drawn from.

**This was dangerous, not untidy.** Everything downstream is indexed by pool POSITION -- the
alive bitmask a `--resume` restores, the per-class digests, the family SHA-256. A resume
against a different pool silently reuses lines, and the invariant `|alive| + sum(sizes) =
|pool|` counts rows and would not notice. A 31 000 -> 35 500 resume run made this way was
**discarded** and the family is being rebuilt in one session. `gamma72.pool_digest` now
fingerprints the pool, `run.py` records it, `build_classes` stores it in the state and REFUSES
to resume without a match, `checkpool.py` asserts it and `regenerate.py` reports it.

### The axis layer was a subset of one shell

`poles18.py` took pole candidates `a = w1 + w2` with `<w1,w2> = 0`. The legality argument is
more general -- for `a` of norm M and `w` of norm m in the same lattice, `|a-w|^2` and `|a+w|^2`
are lattice norms, so `|<a,w>| <= M/2`, and the pole condition `4<a,w>^2 <= 3Mm` is automatic
as soon as `M <= 3m`. So EVERY shell with `m < M <= 3m` is legal, all of it. A_2 has no two
orthogonal roots at all, so **dimension 74 was claiming no poles** while A_2's norm-6 shell is
legal and gives all six. Sweeping both legal shells with a deterministic seeded search gives
**374 poles against 248**, and dimensions 74-80 rise by +6, +2, 0, +2, +20, +20, +76. The
enumeration is checked against each root lattice's theta series -- which caught that E_6's
norm-6 shell has **720** vectors, not the 936 in circulation; confirmed twice, from the E_6
Cartan matrix by Fincke-Pohst and from four different A_2 complements inside E_8.

The same limitation applies at k = 9..23, whose layers run at 0.27% (k = 23) to 40% (k = 12) of
their ceiling tau(k). It is worth nothing there: dimension 95's whole axis layer is 248 points
against a claim of 6.4e9.

### What was checked and did NOT move

* **lambda(12) = 378.** It is half the largest antipodally symmetric 60-degree code of R^12, and
  the record being odd (841) only rules out taking all of it -- a symmetric SUBSET could have
  beaten the lattice's 756. Of Cohn's 841 points exactly **36 have their antipode in the set**,
  so that route gives lambda = 18. Worth 480 000 in dimension 60 had it gone the other way.
* **The units heuristic `2T + P`** that picks the cap-direction configuration agrees with the
  actual gain `4S(T) + 2(S(T+P) - S(T))` in every one of k = 9..23.
* **A better P_48 class family** is capped by the construction's own ceiling at +1.13% (dim 61),
  +0.49% (dim 60) and under a tenth of a per cent below dimension 56. `ceiling.py`'s argument
  ("94-105% of the gap is chance overlap between random images") closes the IMAGES route only;
  a greedy family over the whole 26 208 000-line set is not limited that way, but the ceiling is.
* **Cohn's table**, re-fetched 2026-08-25: dimensions 24-28, 38, 39, 43-48 and 72 all unchanged.
  `[62,26,16]` and `[63,27,16]` re-read on codetables.de with their construction chains;
  `[62,27]` and `[63,28]` still read d = 15. `A(39,8,8) = 3324` re-read on Brouwer's page.

### Independently reproduced, sharing no code with the repository

The extremal theta series and all four one-point distributions (so `tau(71) >= 2 603 658 750`
exactly); the Leech lattice from the Golay code and all eight cross-section counts the k-point
LP is validated against; **the whole k-point programme rebuilt from scratch** -- own cell
enumeration, own Wick moments, own marginals, antipodal and translation constraints -- which
reproduces the cell and equation counts the repository states (9/61/369/1681 cells, 6/36/161/581
moment equations at k = 1..4) and gives

    dim 71   2 603 658 750.0   claimed 2 603 658 750   exact
    dim 70   1 249 778 250.0   claimed 1 249 778 250   exact
    dim 69     627 822 179.8   claimed   627 822 180   the certificate is the ceiling
    dim 68     361 275 480.0   claimed   361 275 480   RAISED 2026-08-26 to exactly this

so the certified values are valid lower bounds and, at k <= 3, essentially tight against the
programme itself; the dimension-27 configuration over Z[sqrt2,sqrt3,sqrt6] (its points
have THREE different norms -- 32, 48 and 432 -- so the test is `4<x,y>^2 <= |x|^2|y|^2`, not a
common-norm comparison); every cap-direction partition for k = 9..23 and the record ones at
k = 9, 10, 14; the Gamma_72 Gram's unimodularity and the exhibited D_4 four-tuple at the
Hermite floor -- and the claimed dimension-68 basis and the dimension-69 A_3 Gram are both
found among the 24 roots that four-tuple generates, which is the realizability the withdrawn
section 40 could not get.

**The calibration, re-derived by hand.**  total(24+k) = 196560 + 2*248*(|W| - M) + poles,
with the parts as triples.py tabulates them:

    k  dim  |W|  parts      |W|-M   gain    poles   total    Cohn
    1   25    2   0T + 1P       1     496       2   197058   197056   +2
    2   26    6   2T + 0P       4    1984       6   198550   198550   MATCH
    3   27   12   4T + 0P       8    3968      12   200540   200044   +496
    4   28   24   8T + 0P      16    7936      24   204520   204520   MATCH
    5   29   40  12T + 2P      26   12896      40   209496   209496   MATCH
    6   30   72  24T + 0P      48   23808      72   220440   220440   MATCH
    7   31  126  42T + 0P      84   41664     126   238350   238350   MATCH

Five exact reproductions of Cohn's table, and the two differences are this project's own two
claims.  The parts cover the direction set exactly in every row (3T + 2P = |W|).  This is the
arithmetic behind 39 of the 47 claims, and it is right.

### Two new checks in the suite

* `audit.py` step **4c**: no claim exceeds a PROVEN upper bound. Steps 1-4b ask whether a claim
  is an improvement; this asks whether it is possible at all, against the upper-bound column of
  Cohn's table -- the only place in the repository that column is read. Tightest: dimension 25
  at 74.4% of 265 006.
* `verify_capdirs.py`, which certifies `lambda(k) = tau(k)/2` for dimensions 57-63, was written
  and never wired into `run_all.py`. It is now.

Running the k-point programme as a MAXIMISATION says what the cross-section could possibly be:

| dim | k | claimed | LP maximum | room |
